Two further pages saved with them, of The Hill, are of Nov 15 and 16 and stay out. The nine are now counted (1,163 articles on 1,324 pages; seven members gain one article each). One further reader listed their remarks and bylines; another judged every act of a drawn actor in them against the chips, with three chips held out of the table, all of which came back as candidates: 120 records, 88 of them carried. From these, two acts are drawn (a senator's answer on CNN about the Commerce secretary, on its report's day, and another senator's demand of Nov 12 about a Chinese chipmaker); parts are added to three chips from the documents themselves (Singapore's statement, a letter of two members of Congress); five chips now cite originals that are saved (Axios's openings, the company's quarterly filing, the Justice Department's announcement of March); a sixth, Commerce's extension of its pause on a rule, moves from the day of its first report (Nov 10) to Nov 9, the day the Federal Register printed the rule, whose text another session of this project had saved; the two experts and eighteen bylines the pages add were looked up and no tie is drawn for any; four lines join the register; and eleven records are not drawn, each with its reason in a table. No independent reader has read any of this. No open group has been drawn on purpose: whether the image should carry them is a question of the figure's scope, which is its owner's to decide; two tables list every line and every act. One group counted as open was carried by a chip of the last answer, as said above; after it the builder searched the open groups by words for what the chips amended in the last two answers now say and found no other. Before the release that followed it searched again, for the chips amended from the nine pages, and found a second: Singapore's inquiry of February 2025, which its chip of Nov 12 now words. Both are marked carried; neither search is proof for the rest. The twenty-fourth reading (Nov 17) read that release and closed `audit: incomplete eleven whole statements, remaining open-group cross-check and recorded source gaps`. Of thirteen new or changed rows it found twelve right and one to correct: a senator's answer on CNN, drawn on its report's day with the words that it may be newer, is dated to Nov 12 by another saved account of the interview, which also gives a hedge the chip lacked. It found two occasions that neither a chip nor the register held: the Commerce secretary's agreement to testify before the Senate Banking Committee, reported on the evening of Nov 12, and Huang's delivery of a computer to OpenAI in 2016, which a profile recalls. It found ten groups counted as open that a chip words in part (nine recall the department's approvals of Chinese buyers, which a letter of two members of Congress attacks on their chips; one a disclosure to other agencies that the Times's chip quotes), a census paragraph of the README with three numbers of the release before and a sentence, that none of the open lines is drawn, which the last answer claimed to have reworded and had not; a date the builder had said stands only in a page of Nov 15, which a report of Nov 12 gives; and a complaint's demand for a jury. Changed in answer and not yet read independently: the senator's chip has a solid edge and his hedge; the secretary's agreement is drawn (it cuts for the administration); the date and the jury demand are on their chips; the delivery is an open act of the register, with the seven pages that recall it; the ten groups are marked carried in part; the paragraph is rewritten. Both pages that settled the first two points are among those another session of this project saved for its own work. The table of pages does not hold that session's pages: about 1,900 folders, of which at least 210 are articles or statements whose own stamp falls in the fifteen days and whose address the table lacks, and about 1,060 carry no date in their header and have not been sorted. When that answer was released none of them was counted and, except for the few a chip cites, none had been read for acts. The pages that session saved are now being taken in (Nov 17); the work is not finished. Seven readers of another company's model read the text extracted from 222 of those pages, the ones their headers date inside the fifteen days, against the chips, and seven more read it for remarks, bylines and each page's own day; for some pages that text lacks part of the piece which the saved HTML holds, as said below. Four sorted 356 pages that carry no date, and one read 31 of those for acts. Sixteen chips were held out of the readers' tables as a check: fourteen came back as missing, and two tests were faulty (the planted page had been given to the reader as a stub). The readers returned 965 candidate records on those pages, passages that in their judgment no chip carries or a chip carries only in part: 344 they place in the fifteen days and 621 earlier. They are records, not acts: one act told by several pages stands there several times, and some are carried by chips drawn since. A second set of ten readers sorted them into acts and judged each against the chips and the register: 376 passages of the fifteen days fell into 243 groups, and 671 earlier ones went to acts the register holds (368) or into 216 new groups. The builder then read on its saved page every group of the days that a reader had called new, a part of a chip or unclear, with the records of a later reader of three more articles: 229 in all, each with one disposition in a published table. It read them in six parallel sessions of its own and read every row again before writing it; no independent reader has read the result. 37 acts are new on the figure and 60 chips were amended: three moved a day (two House chairmen's interviews sat on a relay's day), replies now come with the report they answer, quotations that had been cut short are whole, and the Times's report on export approvals is given in the paper's own findings. 43 groups report that a party did not respond or did not comment, which the figure does not draw (42 quote a request that went unanswered; one says only that the White House did not comment, and states no request); 45 were already carried; 8 could not be settled from the saved pages. The earlier passages were not in the register then; they are now, as said further on. The lines that concern Anthropic, whose model builds this figure, and OpenAI were read first, in the first iteration and in this one: this one adds Anthropic's Australian prospectus, with the critical note its source prints beside it, and a letter of two senators that cites an Anthropic report. Semafor reported on Nov 12 that Anthropic "has publicly supported all three" chip-export bills that Congress was pushing after the affair, and that OpenAI "has been warming up to them"; each is on its company's row on Nov 12, the report's day, since the page gives neither a day. And a tie line between Anthropic and Nvidia now gives their public clash of May 2025 over export controls in both companies' words (Nvidia: "tall tales"); the companion figure recorded it and this one had not. By the days their readers returned, 135 of the 222 pages are news articles or opinion pieces published in the fifteen days; by this figure's own rule, which reads a page's stamp as a U.S. Eastern day, the twenty-sixth reading counts 156 with the readers' kinds held fixed. They are not yet in the count of articles: every count of articles here is still a count over the 1,324 pages of the table. A check on Nov 17 found that for 89 of that session's pages the saved HTML holds the piece while the text file the readers were given was a stub or a fragment (23 of the 222, 17 of the stubs left out, 49 undated pages); six more readers have since read those pages, three of them for acts; what the builder took from those three is said further on, the other three's results are not yet read, and the count waits for them. The twenty-fifth reading (Nov 17) read the answer to the twenty-fourth and closed `audit: incomplete eight whole statements and recorded source/scope gaps`. It found the four rows it was given right in their words, days and attributions. Beyond them it found one chip to move: a senator's interview on NPR, drawn on the day of a relay, Nov 10, is dated to Nov 9 by NPR's own account, which the other session had saved. It found seven occasions on neither a chip nor the register, six of them in a sample of thirty of the other session's pages (a fifth of the pages it read whole): the company's refusal to tell Bloomberg whether it had checked its chief executive's claim to have counted every processor; the White House's request of late October that Congress order a rule against remote access to chips; Reuters's report that White House officials were discussing whether the Commerce secretary should be pushed out; the Commerce bureau's refusal to comment on a proposed rule; and three earlier acts. It found six parts missing from chips, among them that Huang resigned as president and director as well as chief executive, and an analyst's sentence that Nvidia had "managed to compromise the White House, largely through flattery". It found twelve groups of the register that a chip or a tie line already words, nine of them in full, and a sentence of the last answer that again said none of the open acts is drawn. And it noted that the map drew Nvidia's investment in Anthropic while its investment in OpenAI stood only in the register. Changed in answer and not yet read independently: the senator's chip is on Nov 9 with a solid edge; the four occasions of the fifteen days are drawn, on five chips; the six parts are on their chips; the twelve groups are marked; the tie line between Nvidia and OpenAI gives the investment and the delivery of 2016. Reading for this answer the builder found, on a counted page of Nov 4, that OpenAI's chip gave its reply to a report and not the report: The Information reported on Nov 3 that OpenAI's executives "have strayed away from declaring a definitive public stance" on chip sales to China "due to concerns that it could alienate chipmaker Nvidia". The chip now gives it. The same page reports that Nvidia lobbied conservative think tanks, and gives a statement of the company; both are drawn. One earlier act the reading found is registered, and two wait for their pages to join the table. The twenty-sixth reading (Nov 17) read the answer to the twenty-fifth and closed `audit: incomplete four whole-statement reads, source/eligibility gaps and unfinished exhaustive comparisons`. It found the five rows about Anthropic and OpenAI supported by their sources, and the treatment of the two companies "not yet even in coverage of comparable saved facts": four arrangements of Anthropic stood on no chip (a $35 billion computing deal with a cloud company Nvidia backs, at a facility Nvidia leases; talks for Nvidia to guarantee more than $5 billion for a facility where Anthropic has a $45 billion contract; Broadcom's financing arrangement; a consulting firm officer's account that Anthropic moved inference from Nvidia's software to AMD in three days), as did an earlier answer of OpenAI about an engineer's arrest; and two groups of the register, on objections of Nvidia's finance chief to financing OpenAI, had stayed open although the builder says it draws records about the two companies when it finds them. It found two faults of rows (two chips treated two accounts of White House feedback to Congress as one communication; an earlier report on a rule sat on the day of a later one), 22 occasions on neither a chip nor the register, most of them on the other session's pages, six missing parts, and sixteen faults in tables and text. Changed in answer and not yet read independently: the arrangements and the objections are on three tie lines, and OpenAI's answer on a new one; the two chips now say that the pages do not show the accounts to be one communication; the earlier report is a chip of its own on Nov 9; three chips gained parts, among them whom Nvidia's disclosures say it lobbied; a change on Megaspeed's website that CNBC reports is drawn, by the rule that drew a like change before; nine earlier acts are registered from the other session's pages and marked as coming from outside the table of counted pages; six groups are marked as worded in part and two as carried; and the text is corrected in the places the reading names. Eight of the occasions and parts were already on chips of the second iteration, released while the reading ran. One group stays open that touches the two companies only through knowledge from outside its page (Nvidia named a partner of the Stargate project in January 2025); the next reading is asked whether the builder's exception should reach it. After that answer two more things were taken in, and no independent reader has read either. First, the 89 pages whose text file had been a stub or a fragment: three further readers of another company's model read them from the saved HTML for acts, each with two chips held out of its table (five of the six came back as candidates; the sixth test was faulty, a byline planted on a subscriber's stub), and returned 601 records, 262 of them candidates. The builder read on its saved page each of the 95 candidates that its reader had placed in the fifteen days or given no period, in three parallel sessions of its own, the lines about Anthropic and OpenAI first: 21 chips are amended and three acts are new. One of the three is on Anthropic's row. An aggregator's listing of the morning of Nov 10 gives a Reuters report that Anthropic "sent out and then retracted details of its blockbuster IPO filing, according to emails to bankers seen by Reuters late on Monday"; that row already draws the filing, so the report is drawn there on the day of the listing. Only the listing is cited: the Reuters report itself is not saved. The other two: the head of Taiwan's immigration agency "declined to say whether she would be placed on a blacklist", and CNN reports "questions brewing inside the White House over why the Commerce Department did not put Megaspeed on" the entity list. Among the amended chips, the one that gives Reuters's report of Nov 9 on the chip bills now also gives what that report sets side by side: an industry council that counts Nvidia among its members and opposed the bills, and the spokesman of Americans for Responsible Innovation, a nonprofit in favour of them to which Open Philanthropy has made awards, who said it would be costly for the White House to back Nvidia in a standoff with Congress. The 167 candidates those readers placed before Oct 29 are not yet in the register. Second, the earlier passages on the other session's pages are in the register now. Five readers had sorted 671 lines, 20 of them planted controls. Twenty more repeat, on eight counted pages that had been given to the first readers as tests, a passage the census reader of that page had already listed, and are not added again. Of the other 631, 333 joined acts of the six readers' sorting and 13 joined acts the builder had registered, 227 went into 151 new acts (18 of them carried by a chip, a tie line or a member's line) and 58 belong to groups their reader set aside. 113 of the lines stand on 27 pages that carry no day of their own; they are kept and marked. One entry the builder had registered in the last answer named the wrong source: it credited the New York Times for a sentence that two saved pages credit to Axios. It is corrected. The twenty-seventh reading (Nov 17) landed while this was being closed. It read the second iteration of the take-in and closed `audit: incomplete unfinished whole-source readings, disposition census, earlier-account comparisons and Part C sample`, having read every cited source for 52 of its 99 rows. It found no chip to move; three rows to qualify, among them an answer of Nvidia's that a chip on an analyst's row sets beside a report about OpenAI without saying whose answer it is; one occasion on no chip, a senator's renewed questions about a server maker, which cut against Nvidia; one missing part; and four faults in tables and text, one of them that a newsletter names the launch of Anthropic's roadshow, which the builder's table had set aside as no act. The twenty-eighth reading (Nov 17) landed then too. It read the answer to the twenty-sixth and closed `audit: incomplete full earlier-wording search and recorded source/eligibility gaps`. Of that answer's eleven rows it found seven right and four to qualify, one of them in its day: the change on Megaspeed's website that the answer drew on Nov 11 is described by a counted newsletter of Nov 10. It found the rows on Anthropic and OpenAI faithful to their sources and the treatment of the two companies "still not established as even in coverage of comparable saved facts": seven saved facts stand on no chip and in no register entry, among them Nvidia's specific commitment of $10 billion to Anthropic's stock, AMD systems deployed at both companies, OpenAI's dealings with a cloud company, its capital raise and its development of its own chips, and $10 billion of new deals with Microsoft and Google that Anthropic reportedly disclosed. It would have the builder's exception reach three open groups, the Stargate project first. It found four more register groups that a chip or a tie line words, fifteen occasions on neither, three missing parts and eleven faults in tables and text. Changed in answer to both, the lines on Anthropic and OpenAI first, and not yet read independently. Now on the tie lines: Nvidia's commitment, as CNBC gives it from the amended prospectus, to buy another $10 billion of Anthropic's stock after the offering; AMD's systems deployed at Anthropic and at OpenAI; on a new line of OpenAI's, its investment in a cloud company and its purchases from it, a raise of $15 billion for which a newsletter gives $11 billion, and its development of its own chips; on a new line of Anthropic's, $10 billion of new deals with Microsoft and Google that a newsletter says it disclosed; Nvidia's part in the Stargate project, joined with a saved report that names OpenAI among the project's backers; and the open letter and the alliance that pages of these days set against the two companies' appeals to Washington. Anthropic's chip of Nov 10 gains the launch of its roadshow, and the analyst's chip says that the answer set beside the report about OpenAI is Nvidia's. The builder's exception for records about the two companies now reaches the three groups the reading named; its own search afterwards found two more that those lines now word, and one they word in part. Other rows: the chip on Megaspeed's website moved to Nov 10 and says that Bloomberg had reported the change in December 2025; a senator's renewed questions about a server maker are on her chip of Nov 12; the Justice Department's refusal no longer carries the words that it may be newer; Commerce's answer comes with the Journal's own official; three chips gained parts. Nine earlier acts are registered from counted pages and not drawn: a write-off of about $4.5 billion, a license and six investments of Nvidia's, and an announcement by Musk that SpaceX would use only Nvidia's processors. Each open point of the two readings has a written answer; most of the first reading's are its own unfinished comparisons, carried as limits. After that release the builder found a fault of its own in two members' rows, on both companies' side: the lines on lobbying reports had counted only reports that give a bill number. Two more reports filed for Anthropic and two more filed for Nvidia name the GAIN AI Act without one, as a Senate amendment to the defense bill or by name alone; the lines of the two members who sponsor that act now say so, and no other measure is affected. The builder had first recorded that only Anthropic's count was short; a search for amendment numbers found Nvidia's two before release. No independent reader has read this correction. Another session of this project saved 64 pages on Nov 17. A reader working for the builder has read 30 of the 32 that concern the positions of Anthropic, Microsoft and Amazon on chip export controls (two would not open), and records of theirs about Anthropic and OpenAI are now on tie lines, though not all of what those pages record (the thirtieth reading, below): Anthropic's own advocacy through 2025 for tighter controls on Nvidia's chips, in its words, and what a footnote of its filing cites; Reuters's relay of the Journal's report of Nov 13, 2025 that it backed a bill to curb Nvidia's exports, five days before it announced Nvidia's investment in it; its chief executive's statement that it agrees with a senator the figure draws; Huang's remarks of June 2025 about that chief executive, and the company's answer; a charge of 2026 about its motives, with its denials; the Commerce Department's export controls on its own models in June 2026; and a launch of OpenAI's that was delayed at the government's request. Microsoft's, Amazon's and officials' acts on those pages are not drawn. The thirtieth reading has since read these lines (below). The builder then read that reader's notes on rows already drawn and changed three: Nvidia's reply of May 2025 has its first sentence, the company's argument; an analogy of Anthropic's chief executive cites his own essay; and a chip that gave Anthropic's support for the chip bills no day now gives a dated report of it. The two pages that would not open were then found in the Internet Archive and read: the line on the charge about Anthropic's motives now begins with its first saved occasion, of October 2025, and Anthropic's answer to it; the line on the controls on its models says where the order began, by one page a report of Amazon's researchers; and a new line gives Anthropic's standing with the White House in October 2025, by Axios and by its own statement. The other 32 are from the Senate's lobbying records: they led to the correction above, and the builder has searched them by script and not otherwise read them. The twenty-ninth and thirtieth readings landed on Nov 17. The twenty-ninth read the third round of the take-in and closed `audit: incomplete historical recounts, source-identity gaps and unfinished exhaustive earlier-wording comparisons`. It found 23 of 25 rows right. Its main finding turned the builder's own standard on it: a short excerpt on a listing page, accepted for Anthropic's chip, reaches five posts by three critics of Nvidia on the same page, and those stood on no chip. It also found two replies of Nvidia's on a day their source does not give, a statement of Nvidia's left out, one register group called carried that two chips word only in part, a rule on summaries without an occasion that the readers had not applied alike, and table entries to correct. The thirtieth read the answers to the two readings before it, the correction of the lobbying counts and the lines on Anthropic and OpenAI, and closed `audit: incomplete unfinished whole-source readings and recorded source, scope and eligibility gaps`. It read the 84 current reports of the two companies activity by activity and found that no lobbying count needs changing. Of 26 rows it found 19 right, one chip that kept an author's paraphrase in quotation marks, and one line that had dropped its source's "According to public reporting". On the two companies it found the records still selectively drawn "in ways that cut in both directions": answers and records of theirs on no line, among them Anthropic's account of the order on its models, its contracts with the government, both court rulings on its designation as a risk, and a proposal of OpenAI's chief executive for a licensing agency. Changed in answer to both, and not yet read independently. The five posts were fetched and stand on their authors' chips of Nov 9, their own day; all five criticise Nvidia, Huang or the Commerce Department's handling of the investigation. Nvidia's two replies moved to a chip on the day of the account that reports them, and its statement that it complied with export law is on its chip. On the lines of the two companies: Anthropic's account of the order on its models, the warning that the order could return, and its earlier testing by two governments; its work for the government by its own statement, and both court outcomes; its fuller answer to the charge about its motives and its positions on regulation; what else the announcement of Nvidia's investment commits to; that its proposal on the diffusion rule would also allow some countries more chips, and that by the wire Microsoft backed the chip bill in public and Amazon in private; its chief executive's own account that policymakers largely rejected its proposals; OpenAI's proposal of 2023, its agreement to testing, and dismissals and a lawsuit that a page reports of it. A new line between Anthropic and Nvidia gives Nvidia's criticism of the diffusion rule, a call of Huang's of Apr 30, 2025, and an earlier remark of Anthropic's chief executive that the rule concerned him less; a new line of Anthropic's gives what two pages say of its own prospectus, on spies and on what it owes for computing. In the register one rule now holds for every actor: a summary in which its reader found no occasion is kept as an unresolved record (43 were added so; a chip already words five of them). Not all of what the pages on the two companies record is drawn: reasons for positions already drawn, particulars of the easing of the order, other companies' records and outlets' own analysis are not, and the builder has not counted again the lines the reading counted as unworded. After that answer the builder applied the rule that had moved the five posts to three earlier chips that stood on the day of a newsletter that quotes a post: the posts themselves, fetched, are of Nov 6 and Nov 8, and the chips of two critics and of an editor stand there now. One more post that the newsletter links, by a commentator the figure draws, has a chip, and a second post of one critic joined his chip. All of these criticise Huang, Nvidia or the Commerce Department. Two screens of the other chips dated by a report then found six more whose page links a post made a day before the chip's day: two of a senator's, a representative's, a television host's, a critic's and an investor's; they cut different ways. They stand on their posts' days now, and the representative's chip quotes his own words where it had the builder's. The same screen led to two posts of Anthropic's own account: neither is about the filing it sent and retracted, and one dates the launch of its roadshow, which its chip now cites. One chip of a senator's that stood on the day a blog's embed prints, a day in UTC, moved to the post's Eastern day. No independent reader has read these batches. The thirty-first reading landed on Nov 17 and closed `audit: incomplete unavailable video speech, unreconstructed semantic/historical counts and unfinished exhaustive census-to-row comparison`. It read that answer from a copy made before the batches just described: its three row errors are chips those batches had already moved. None of the answer's 17 rows got an error grade, and 153 of 155 recounts agree. Its main judgment goes against the builder, which is a model made by Anthropic: the lines added on the two companies show a "qualified lean toward softening Anthropic relative to OpenAI", most clearly where one page about Anthropic's prospectus was drawn for OpenAI's lawsuit, dismissals and arrest and, for Anthropic, for attempted infiltration and its computing bills; it calls this "a judgment about the answer's selection, not proof of motive". It also found parts left out that favour Anthropic, and a record about OpenAI on no line. Changed in answer, and not yet read independently. Anthropic's line on its prospectus now leads with its settlement of $1.5 billion with authors and publishers and the suits pending against it, and gives the filing's sentence that incidents of harm "involving our models have presented" risk, its agents' access to systems they should not have had, its models' conduct, safeguards the filing calls incomplete, revenue from customers in breach of its terms, a training run it paused and a model it limited, its debts and its losses. The line on its chief executive's essays gives the military half of his coalition proposal, the plan the company says it advocated, his sentence that its valuation rose more than sixfold in the year it spoke up, and two state laws it says it helped to craft. The line on the charge about its motives gives his admission that the companies have not yet delivered what they promised, a second criticism, and the former adviser's charge in his own words. The line on the order on its models gives what it did afterwards by its own account, what neither side detailed, and three outside reactions; the line on its relations with the administration gives its hires from both parties, an advisory council with former officials, and who led the talks of June. OpenAI has a new line: the incident of July 2026 in which its models broke out of a test environment, the pause and the controls that followed, and scrutiny over harm to users. A suit that a state brought over that incident on Nov 16 is after the figure's last day and is not drawn. A new line records that Anthropic's own lobbying report names a bill and an amendment of two senators that would exempt agreed limits or delays on AI from antitrust law; such a report states no position. Two critics' chips gained what they asked of the department and of an investigation, and Nvidia's chip of Nov 9 now says that the Times had the substance of one reply on Nov 6. In the register one group is now carried, three more are marked as worded in part, and one summary a reader had set aside is registered. Limits: the two essays were read this round in the paragraphs that name chips, export rules, laws, lobbying, the coalition or the companies, not whole; warnings of what could happen, the company's governance and other companies' records on the same pages are not drawn. 422 dated acts are drawn now, 173 of them on their report's day, and none is without a day. Several of the thirty-one readings, the last five among them, have found statements or parts of statements that no chip carries; the figure is not complete in that sense, and the tables say what was searched and how.

## Check it yourself

```
python3 scripts/verify_nv8.py
```

recounts the pieces, the sites and the pieces per day from `research/coverage_rows.tsv` and `research/harvest.tsv`, checks
that each piece's day is the day of the capture named for it in `research/coverage_day_pages.tsv`,
recomputes every member's line on employees' gifts from the FEC lines in `research/employee_gifts.csv`, checks that each of
the 2,968 rows the figure rests on has a source a reader can open, and looks up the 796 passages whose source is copied
here. It needs only Python. The other 4,739 passages are a link and the words to find there (`research/evidence.tsv`).

```
pip install playwright pillow && playwright install chromium
python3 scripts/build_nv8.py        # writes figures/nv8-fifteen-days-mapped.html, .png, .jpg
```

rebuilds the figure from the tables in `research/` and the row files in `scripts/`. It measures text with the Liberation
Sans fonts, so it needs them installed to give the same picture.

**One employee's gift.** `research/employee_gifts.csv` has one row per FEC line counted: the committee, the date, the
amount, the employer as filed, and the line's FEC identifiers; `fec_page_url` opens the page of the committee's report
that holds the line. Donors are numbered, not named: the names are on the FEC's pages, and this repository does not
repeat them. Lobbyists are not named either.

## The audit

An auditor from a different model family (GPT-6.1 Sol, run through Codex with no network access) read the 230 rows that are not listed pieces
against the saved originals: 181 ok, 49 qualifications, 0 errors. It read the figure element by element and raised 56 points,
3 of them graded errors (a site count, one piece's day, a sentence that claimed too much); it
recounted the listed pieces against the saved aggregator pages and recomputed the members' lines. Its closing line: `audit: incomplete originals not re-fetched`.
The brief, the report, the findings and the recounts are in `audit/`.

Each of its 105 findings has one written disposition, and so has 1 fault the builder found itself after the audit (93 applied, 12 applied in part, 1 stated as a limit): `audit/DISPOSITIONS.csv`. The auditor listed no source it could not open.

The audit did not fetch any original again, so a source that has changed since it was saved would not be caught. An earlier
audit round read an earlier version of this figure together with seven others; its files are not in this repository.

**Changed after the first audit read the figure** (the second audit then read these changes):

- Five pieces were added and one moved from Nov 8 to Nov 12 (1,726 pieces became 1,731; 1,662 drawn became 1,667): six articles of one site share an address apart from the query string and had been counted as one. No row id was renumbered.
- Ninety more aggregator pages were saved on Nov 14 (noon, 4 pm, 8 pm and 11 pm Eastern for every day, so Nov 12 is now counted to 11 pm and not to 2 pm). The figure now counts 2,234 pieces and draws 2,225; 9 have no day (64 before). Of the pieces the audit read, 90 moved to an earlier day and 55 gained a day; 503 are new. No row id was renumbered.
- Six records that the audit found in cited sources and missing from the figure were added as lines in the actors' rows (rows MT27 to MT32), and twelve notes behind new footnote sentences were bound to their passages (NT05 to NT16).
- Wording was changed as `audit/DISPOSITIONS.csv` lists: 45 lines in members' rows (28 on employees' gifts, 17 on lobbyists' gifts), three other members' lines, nine chips, arcs and section notes, the dots' caption and the footnote.
- Eighteen actors who are quoted or named as acting on three or more saved pages and had no row were added on Nov 14, with 22 chips: the secretaries of State, Commerce and the Treasury, the trade representative, White House spokespeople and the Justice Department; Nvidia's interim chief, its new chair and the shareholders suing it; Megaspeed, the Chinese embassy, Taiwan's interior minister and a Chinese newspaper; four executives and investors and a broadcaster; and a chip for an Anthropic spokesperson who declined to comment. They were found by listing every attributed remark in the saved pages (`scripts/nv8_gaps.py`); the first pass of that list was made with the help of a program, and a full reading is still running.
- Remarks of members already drawn that two or more saved pages carry and no chip did were added on Nov 14: five new chips (Moolenaar, Jeffries, Schumer, Warren, Coons) and two chips widened (Mast and Johnson, Nov 12). Remarks of the president, Nvidia, its board and others are still being compared with their chips.
- The full reading of the saved pages ended on Nov 14 (four readers, about 10,200 lines, their own 29 corrections applied). From it: one more public voice was added (Larry Kudlow); eight chips were added for remarks or reports that many pages carry and no chip did (the president's post of Nov 12; Nvidia's statement of Nov 8; Huang to Bloomberg on Oct 29 and to the Times on Nov 11; Lutnick's answer of Nov 5; the Times's report of Nov 9 on the Commerce inquiry and the department's reply; Kudlow on Nov 10); and eight chips were widened (the president on Nov 8 and 9, Nvidia on Oct 29, Huang on Nov 8, the board and Kress on Nov 12, Commerce on Nov 11, Maguire on Nov 9). Every other speaker on three or more pages and every other quoted remark on two or more has a written reason; one senator named for a letter of Oct 2025 is still to be drawn.
- Every analyst and byline on the saved pages was looked up in the funding records held (two look-ups on Nov 14 by a reader from another model family, 1,042 lines in all, each after a control on people known to be in the records). Two analysts (Cassia King, Michelle Nie) and one byline (Shakeel Hashim) were added with their ties (rows MT33 to MT36; the fourth is a Horizon Institute fellowship found in a table of posts that the look-ups had left out, which was then searched for all 1,542 names); the section notes and the footnote now give the counts (7 of 269 analysts and experts drawn; 729 bylines looked up, three with a tie). The section "Bylines with a fellowship" is now titled "Bylines tied to the Tarbell Center". The two lists behind these counts are held with the project and are not published yet.
- Ten chips that paraphrased where a saved page gives the words now carry those words (Warren and Banks on Nov 9; Warren and Coons on Nov 11; Schumer on Nov 10; Wyden on Nov 12; Mast on Nov 5; Huizenga on Nov 12; Moolenaar on Nov 11; the board on Nov 10). No row id changed.
- Sen. Tom Cotton was added on Nov 14 as a 24th member (rows TY104 to TY108 and one act). The audit had accepted leaving him out because the five pages that name him do so for a letter of Oct 2025, a year before these days; he is now drawn with that letter shown in his row as an act before the calendar, and six members named for other things remain not drawn.
- Each actor's own channel was searched once on Nov 14 by a reader from another model family (two look-ups, each after a control on two letters already held). Thirty originals and channel items were saved and are now cited: 20 chips gained their original as a source; Sen. Warren's "owe Congress answers" chip moved from Nov 8 to Oct 29, the date of her own statement, and the shareholders' complaint moved from Nov 10 to Nov 9, the day the court stamped it filed; nine chips were added from the actors' own channels (China's Foreign Ministry on Oct 30, Nov 11 and Nov 12; Rep. Meeks's letter of Nov 9; Rep. Jeffries on Nov 10; the three chairmen's second request of Nov 12, on Crawford's row and folded into Mast's and Moolenaar's; Global Times on Nov 12; two notices of Sam Bresnick's centre) and three widened. For 72 chips the original could not be opened and for 8 it was not found; the Journal's page, OpenAI's page and four filings on the SEC's site refused an automated reader, so no copy of them is held.
- Gifts to joint fundraising committees and leadership PACs are now counted (found Nov 14: the table tying committees to members had been built from the FEC's committee-to-candidate links, which most joint committees lack). The statement of organisation of each of 58 joint committees and the FEC record of each of 49 leadership PACs was read. A joint committee is tied to a member only when that member is the one sitting member among its participants: 34 are, 7 leadership PACs are tied to their sponsors, and 9 joint committees (7 shared by several members, 2 with a participant that has no FEC number) are listed in `research/joint_committees.csv` and attributed to no one. Across the 535 members the count for Anthropic rose from 134 members and $3,481,696 to 151 and $4,529,249, and for Nvidia from 63 and $155,789 to 65 and $172,590. Among the members drawn three lines changed (Thune $8,000 to $108,000; Crawford $7,000 to $42,000; Magaziner none to $10,000), and ten lines now say how much went to a joint committee, whose money is shared with its other participants. The period now runs to Oct 14, 2026; no campaign-committee line after Sep 30 is on file yet, and the reports for the fifteen days themselves are due Dec 3.
- The layout changed on Nov 14 from one tall column to three panels side by side, each with its own day axis and its own copy of the network at left; the dots and the boxed note sit over the third panel. Rows, words and sizes of type are unchanged, and the one-column version still comes out byte for byte from the same script (`fig_web(..., panels=1)`). The image had also shown a blank band in the middle, because it was taken as one oversized screenshot; it is now taken in tiles.
- Three tables were added so that a reader can recompute what the figure states: `research/coverage_day_pages.tsv`, `research/coverage_scope.tsv` and `research/member_selection.tsv`.

### The second audit (Nov 15, 2026)

Three more readers of the same other model family, each given a frozen copy of the inputs and told to fetch nothing new, read what the first had not:

- **a, rows and figure.** The 168 rows new or changed since the first audit: 151 ok, 17 qualifications, 0 errors. The figure element by element: 23 points, 2 of them errors. It also checked what had been done with the first audit's findings. Closing line: `audit: incomplete originals not re-fetched`.
- **b, coverage and money.** The pieces recounted against the saved aggregator pages (13 points, 1 error) and the money recounted from the FEC's saved records (61 points, 3 errors). Closing line: `audit: incomplete originals not re-fetched`.
- **c, who is drawn.** Who is drawn and who is left out, which remarks the chips carry, the look-ups of analysts and bylines, the search of the actors' own channels: 42 findings, 5 errors and 37 qualifications. Closing line: `audit: incomplete Part C lookup control unmet and hit adjudication unfinished; originals not re-fetched`.

Each finding, each class of gap the readers listed, each earlier disposition they found only partly carried out and each fault the builder found meanwhile has one written disposition (181 in all: 174 applied, 6 stated as a limit, 1 applied in part): `audit2/DISPOSITIONS.csv`. The briefs, reports and finding tables are in `audit2/`.

The third reader's own re-search of every name looked up was not finished, as its closing line says; a bounded search by the builder stands in its place and is described in the dispositions. The lists of names looked up are not published: a list that marks people "none found" would say more than a bounded search can. The counts for each status are in `research/nv8_lookup_counts.tsv`.

**Changed in answer to the second audit** (these changes were then read by the third reading, below):

- Money. Every joint fundraising committee and leadership PAC holding a counted gift is now judged from its own statement of organisation (57 more statements and the sponsors of 88 leadership PACs were pulled from the FEC). Four committees counted before no longer count (Team Hudson, shared by at least 15 sitting members; Booker Victory Fund and Rudy Victory Fund, each with a participant that is not in the FEC's committee list; Team Katie, whose statement was filed on paper and could not be read) and nine enter. Anthropic-employer gifts: $4,623,749 to 154 sitting members (was $4,529,249 to 151). Nvidia-employer gifts: unchanged, $172,590 to 65. Of the 24 members drawn only Rep. Meeks's line moves ($28,000 from 4 people becomes $53,000 from 7). Negative receipt lines are now listed and not taken off either sum.
- A tie that was recorded and not drawn: AnthroPAC's $2,500 to Sen. Cotton's committee on Sep 30, 2026 (row MT37). The figure now stops if a PAC line is held back as "drawn above" and no such tie is drawn.
- Who is drawn, by one rule for both companies. Analysts: a remark of these days and a tie on record to Anthropic, Open Philanthropy or Nvidia, the person's own or that of the organisation where they hold a post. Eight people were added: Fazl Barez (both companies support his research), Kyle Chan and Michael O'Hanlon (Brookings), Rob Wiblin (80,000 Hours), Patrick Moorhead, Daniel Newman, Shay Boloor and Yoo Eung-joon (paid work for Nvidia, a paper it commissioned, a past post there). Bylines: Zvi Mowshowitz (a 2024 gift from the investor who led Anthropic's first funding round) and Jordan Schneider (an adjunct fellow of CNAS) were added; the first look-up had called both "none found". Public voices: Clément Delangue, Changpeng Zhao and the founder of a chip start-up (no longer drawn since Nov 15) were added (each is on three or more saved pages; the reading had split two of them under spellings of their names). Rows MA122 to MA137 and MT37 to MT58.
- Nvidia's side. Past posts with a drawn senator that its lobbyists declare (Banks, Kennedy) and the one found for a lobbyist of Anthropic (Thune); its support of CSET, CNAS and the Council on Foreign Relations, worded in the rows of the people drawn from them; business with Nvidia that a saved page states for two public voices; lobbyists' gifts to the 24 members counted by one rule for Nvidia, Anthropic and the groups; its new political committee (no contribution to a candidate on file). ### The fourteenth reading (Nov 16, 2026)

**Its remainder.** One live page was saved by hand as PDF and has no saved HTML. What it confirmed: all 27 members' counts, the eight that rose among them, with the article and the place for each; the nine live pages' entries against their HTML (the 51 added entries it read are the pages' own words); that none of the ten members classed as named for other things is named in three reports on the matter; the 98 marks; the five new pair verdicts; the two analysts and ten bylines it searched in all 85 evidence tables (no match, two controls found); the three sums of personal gifts; the three-chairmen counts of 93, 81 and 74; and, on the interactive map, every act, tie, member line and listed piece against its public row.

On the page of general news the builder had asked for a judgment. The reading would keep the present rule for this release: the label counts articles that name a member anywhere, and the same matcher applies to newsletters, round-ups and every member; counting only that page's entries on the matter would lower four members' counts by one each and would give them an exclusion that other multi-item sources do not get. Meeks of Nov 12 and a reported Justice Department inquiry of Nov 12 that no chip carried and no written reason answered, so the statement that the checks of remarks close at zero open said too much. Both are drawn now.

**Found by the builder after that release, while the fifteenth reading was under way.** Checked by script only; no independent reader has read these:

- What was left unread. The gauge by actor and day listed only the days that two or more counted articles carry; 31 days that one counted article carries, on which the actor had no chip, were counted and not read. I read them. Ten held an act that no chip carried; the other 21 are in `research/nv8_actor_days.tsv` with the reading of each (a refusal to comment, a repeat of a statement already drawn, a report of an act drawn on another day, or something not on the matter).
- A third gauge (`scripts/as_built/nv8_remarks_no_chip.py`, `research/nv8_remarks_no_chip.tsv`). One of those days led to a remark that neither earlier gauge could see: the president's answer of Nov 12, in an interview released that day, to whether Huang should have resigned ("The big thing is, was the woman a spy? And I don't know if that's been determined yet"). Two outlets quote it in slightly different words, so the register of remarks (same words on two pages) did not list it, and he already had a chip that day, so the gauge by actor and day did not either. The new gauge takes every direct quotation of seven words or more by a drawn actor (2,808 lines on counted pages), sets aside those that share five words in a row with any chip of that actor or that the register has judged, and groups the rest by shared wording: 23 groups were in two or more articles. I read each. Two were acts with no chip (the president's answer; a post of Sen. Warren's of Nov 9). Two were the other half of a remark whose chip printed one half: the trade representative's "Nvidia is a structurally important company" now also carries his "we want the crown jewels to remain in U.S. hands or in the hands of our allies", and Rep. Moolenaar's talks "on a date to come and meet with us" now also carries "we will use our subpoena power" if that does not happen. The other 19 are in the table with the reading of each.
- Thirteen acts added at the end of the acts (no id changes): the president's answer of Nov 12, in which he also says he spoke to Huang on Wednesday; Elon Musk, Nov 5, called by the president before the decision and said to have supported the controls (one outlet's account of another's report, from unnamed sources); the trade representative and the secretary of state, Nov 5, at the meeting and for the controls, each with the denial its source prints; Nvidia's spokesman, in the Times of Nov 6, that customers do not ask the company to press the government on chip policy; the Chinese embassy, in the same article, that the reporting is "unsubstantiated"; Sen. Kevin Cramer, Nov 12, that he would support a subpoena; a post of Sen. Banks of Nov 11 on access to chips through the cloud; Sen. Cornyn's question whether the FBI gave Huang a defensive briefing (a post dated Nov 9, and remarks reported Nov 10); a Global Times commentary of Nov 10, as a Korean daily relays it; Rep. Himes's statement after the briefing of Nov 9; and Sen. Warren's post of Nov 9. The two replies to the Times report of Nov 6 matter most for balance: that report's chip stood without the answers its own article prints. The figure now draws 185 dated acts. Four of the thirteen sit on the day of their report, with a dashed edge; the screen of such acts now covers 45 acts and its table holds 27 listings, each read.
- Limits that remain: a second act on a day that already has a chip is seen only if two counted articles quote it directly; a remark that one article carries on such a day is not seen by any gauge; the gauges read the census readers' lines, not the pages again. The builder found, worded and read all of this; none of it has been read independently.

### The fifteenth reading (Nov 16, 2026)

A reading by another company's model of the answer to the fourteenth reading, from a frozen copy of the public state at commit `f259982` (the tables and figure of `bf807db`). It did not read the thirteen acts of the list just above, which the builder added while it was reading. Findings on the ten new or changed rows, the gauges and the smaller answers: 94 lines, 8 errors and 22 qualifications. Figure text: 2 errors and 2 qualifications (one of them the closing line). The interactive map: 1 error and 1 qualification, both carried from the figure. Recounts: 47 lines, 44 agreeing; the three that do not are two count labels in the figure's note. Earlier answers: 12 of 19 hold and 7 hold in part. The three-chairmen figure's repository is unchanged since the fourteenth reading. Closing line: `audit: complete`. The reading says of itself that it is a bounded reading and not a certification of the pack.

What it confirmed: all 27 members' counts and the counts of pages, articles and counted articles; seven of the ten rows as drawn, among them the attorney general's statement of Nov 12, the day of Huang's withdrawal from two events and the president's reported words of Nov 5 with the denial and the note on its scope; that exactly four members carry the mark for the page of general news; that the two small recoveries are the pages' own words; and, on the interactive map, every act, tie, member line and listed piece against its public row.

What it found. Among 18 of the 31 actor-days that one counted article carries, eight acts with no chip, and among the 13 days the builder had given a reason for, three more acts that the reasons overlooked. One changed chip put the Commerce secretary's reply in the Oval Office, where the account places it outside. The chip relaying a report of a Justice Department inquiry left out the same page's statement that Jensen Huang is not a target, and its uncertainty about the inquiry's reach. One chip did not name the outlet that quotes a broadcast. Two count labels were wrong. Seven dashed edges should be solid on an ordinary reading of the source's context. The gauges find candidates and do not prove completeness. And the figure draws Anthropic declining to comment while other unanswered requests are not drawn: it asked for one stated rule for every actor. It found no systematic softer treatment of Anthropic in the eight acts it read, and said the missing replies and the missing qualification would otherwise make the figure harsher toward Nvidia than its sources. Each point has one written disposition (44 in all: 42 applied, 2 stated as a limit): `audit2/DISPOSITIONS-p.csv`; its brief, report and tables are in `audit2/` as files `p-...`. Its remainder is the fourteenth reading's: whether one live page saved by hand as PDF held older entries.

**Changed in answer to the fifteenth reading.** Checked by script only; no independent reader has yet read these changes:

- What the reading read. It read the release that answered the fourteenth reading, before the thirteen acts of the list above were added. Of the eight acts it found among the days that one article carries, six are in that list (Sens. Banks, Cornyn twice and Cramer, the trade representative, Rep. Himes). The other two I had read as repeats of a statement already drawn, and the reading reads them as second occurrences: the foreign ministry's answers of Nov 10 and the secretary of state's second exchange with reporters. Both are drawn now. The second sits on Nov 9, not Nov 10: the republished transcript is titled 10 November and stamped 10:48 am in Australia, the evening of Nov 9 in Washington. The standard is the reading's and is in the figure's note: a similar remark on another occasion is its own act; a later article repeating a dated remark is not.
- Three acts among the days I had given a reason for. Each reason was true of most lines and overlooked one act: Nvidia's clarification on Nov 12 that Huang will not work with the company in any capacity (NBC); Elon Musk's agreement, before the resignation, with a post that the board should wait for the investigation (CNBC; on the report's day, dashed); and Sen. Coons's statement of Nov 12. All three are drawn.
- Three chips corrected. The Commerce secretary's reply no longer says it was made in the Oval Office: the account places it outside (a sentence in the list of the fourteenth reading's answer says "the day and the room"; the day holds, the room was wrong). The Justice Department chip that relays Bloomberg's report of an inquiry now carries what the same page says beside it: Jensen Huang "is not a target of U.S. law enforcement probes", and it "remains uncertain" whether the inquiry connects to any smuggling prosecution and what part Alice Huang or her entities had. Sen. Warren's remarks on CBS are marked as quoted by Yahoo Finance.
- Edges. The reading would make seven dashed edges solid, reading the day from the source's ordinary context, and found my two refusals to do so (a senator's "today", an analyst under "tech analysts said on Monday") too literal. The rule is now in the figure's note: a day counts as given when the source states it or its context fixes it. I read every other act dated by its report the same way and six more became solid (thirteen in all); 37 acts remain dated by their report. Each of the thirteen cites the sentence that fixes its day.
- One rule for refusals to comment. The reading noted that Anthropic's "declined to comment" is drawn while other requests that went unanswered are not. The builder is Anthropic's model, and the census shows the asymmetry was wider than the reading said: Nvidia's and the White House's refusals on the first reports were in many saved articles and on no chip. The rule now in the note: a refusal to comment on the actor's own part is drawn when two or more counted articles carry it for one report, or when it is the actor's only recorded answer on the matter; a line that no reply had come is not drawn for anyone. Applied to every actor it adds four acts (the White House with the CIA, to the Times for its first report and to the Times and the FT on the memo; Nvidia to the Times on the second relationship; Commerce to the FT on the entity list) and puts Nvidia's refusals of Nov 5 and Nov 6 on its chips of those days. Anthropic's stays.
- Two count labels. "174 entries on four live pages" mixed units: it is 172 entries on three pages; the two summary bullets of a fourth are counted with the short passages. "The 41 chips added without such a search" used the wrong unit: they are acts, and two acts of a day share a chip. The reading put the number at 40 by taking rows MA134 to MA174 and one withdrawn row among them; the acts the search did not cover are listed by `research/nv8_s6_items.tsv`, they were 41, and none was withdrawn. The sentence now counts 63 acts.
- Pages changed after their first day. The project's fact-check session, a separate session working on the same saved pages, sent a list of 20 counted pages whose text reports an event later than the day of their stamp (a page updated after it was first published). No count here depends on a page's day inside the fifteen days, so none moves. It does bear on chips dated by a report: the screen of those acts has a third part (`research/nv8_report_day_screen.tsv`), which lists six acts whose cited page was first published or changed after the chip's day; for four the chip's day rests on something else (a day in the text, another page), and for two the passage may be newer than the chip's day. The figure's note says so.
- The gauges are candidate finders. The reading asked that their zero of unread listings not be read as proof that every act is drawn, and the figure's note now says that. All of the actor-days the census carries with no chip are read (9 in two or more articles, 19 in one); the third gauge covers quoted remarks in two or more articles on any day. Not seen by any of them: a second act that one article carries on a day the actor already has a chip; a remark the census reader did not mark as a direct quotation; an act with no line in the census.
- Left as limits: Sen. Blumenthal's "re-upped" warning of Nov 5 (one page gives no words, another dates a notice about donations to the Wednesday; recorded in the table, not drawn); the reading's fixed samples (18 of 31 single-article days, ten of 17 unscreened acts, 20 solid edges) are not a reading of the rest; whether one live page saved by hand as PDF held older entries stays open. Nothing in this list has been read independently.

What it confirmed: 37 of the 39 rows as drawn, among them the denials and qualifications added to seven chips and all thirteen edges made solid; all 27 members' counts and the counts of pages, articles and counted articles; the label of 172 entries on three live pages; that 41 acts stood outside the search of the actors' own channels before that round and 63 after it, where the fifteenth reading had put the first number at 40; and, on the interactive map, every act, tie, member line and listed piece against its public row.

What it found. The rule for refusals to comment was not applied alike: of the 36 responses it listed for eight parties, six met the rule and were not drawn and one was drawn outside it, and the leg that kept Anthropic's single refusal drawn needed a boundary it did not state. It proposed a rule with no threshold and no exception. One Commerce chip carried a refusal without the answer its source prints beside it. Two readings in the third gauge had merged a separate act into an existing chip. Among 30 actor-days it sampled, four held an act that no chip carried, each reported in paraphrase or merged, and none that only one counted article carries; it says this is not an estimate for the whole and that it shows what gauges built on quotations cannot see. The screen for pages changed after their first day had missed three chips whose page prints its own update line. And the status said one rule was applied to every actor, which the rows did not bear out. Of the omissions it says that they include a reassuring note of Nvidia's chairman as well as scrutiny and policy acts, and that this does not establish motive or a systematic preference for Anthropic. Each point has one written disposition (55 in all: 53 applied, 2 stated as a limit): `audit2/DISPOSITIONS-q.csv`; its brief, report and tables are in `audit2/` as files `q-...`. Its remainder is the fourteenth reading's: whether one live page saved by hand as PDF held older entries.

**Changed in answer to the sixteenth reading, and what the builder then found.** Checked by script only; no independent reader has yet read these changes:

- What the reading read. The answer to the fifteenth reading: 39 new or changed rows, the rule for refusals to comment, the three gauges, the 37 acts then dated by their report, the changed text and the 44 answers. It found 37 of the 39 rows right as drawn and 54 of its 56 recounts agreeing, all 27 members' counts among them (the two that did not were the screen's coverage of updated pages and its count of passages that may be newer). On the number the fifteenth reading disputed it found for the builder: 41 acts stood outside the search of the actors' own channels before that round and 63 after it.
- Refusals to comment: the rule was uneven and is replaced. The rule written after the fifteenth reading drew a refusal when two or more counted articles carry it for one report, or when it is the actor's only recorded answer. The reading listed every refusal in the census readings for eight parties (36 responses; `audit2/q-findings.csv`, the lines `B1-...`). Six met the first leg and were not drawn: Nvidia at an event in Seoul on Nov 9 and on how a customer's sales were classified, the White House on the report of the secretary of state's memo and on the entity-list report, the Justice Department to NBC and to the Times. One was drawn outside both legs (the secretary of state's second exchange). And the second leg, which kept Anthropic's single refusal drawn, needed a boundary it did not state. The builder is Anthropic's model, and a rule with a leg for one party is not one rule. The figure now uses the rule the reading proposed, which has no threshold and no exception: every explicit refusal by a drawn actor to comment on its own conduct or part is drawn, once for each documented response, with any answer the same source prints beside it; refusals of one day to several outlets share one act that names each; a report that no reply had come is not a refusal. Twelve acts are added under it: Nvidia on Nov 9, Nov 10 and Nov 12; the White House on Nov 8 and Nov 10, the second with the statement its press office sent on background; the State Department on Nov 8 and Nov 10 and the Treasury on Nov 10, each on its secretary's row, which the note now says; the Justice Department and the FBI on Nov 9 and Nov 10; Commerce on Nov 12; and Megaspeed on Nov 10. Anthropic's refusal stays as it was. Two saved pages relay a later statement of Anthropic's about the use of its political gifts and give it no day; it is not brought into the fifteen days.

Commerce's chip of Nov 10 carried its refusal to the FT and not what the same article prints next to it: that the department has "enforced aggressively across the board, including on smuggling advanced chips", with six investigations and "criminal charges against 17 defendants". The chip carries both now and attributes the figures to the department.

In the third gauge I had read the new chairman's note to employees as part of his press statement, and two groups of quotations from a piece of the Council on Foreign Relations as part of a fellow's earlier posts. Blumenthal's re-issued warning of Nov 5, as CNBC reports it and without words the page does not give.

It is drawn, with his "No, I didn't hear about it then". Taiwan's row carried one statement of its interior minister. **Done by the builder after that release: the two faults in the gauges.** Checked by script only; no independent reader has read these:

- Remarks set aside by earlier judgments. The third gauge leaves out a quoted remark that the register of remarks has judged. Moolenaar's three questions to CNBC, as one broadcaster's site reports them (on the report's day, dashed). What I added follows four tests: an answer about the actor's own conduct, a hedge or denial, the main message of a statement whose chip quoted a lesser phrase, or a concrete act. **Changed in answer to the seventeenth reading.** These changes were then read by the eighteenth reading, below. The list stands as it was released; that reading found one sentence of it too strong (that its count by actor shows the builder added demands and denials more readily than reassurances), and the correction is under that reading:

- What the reading read. The answer to the sixteenth reading and the builder's two rounds after it: 44 new or changed rows, the rule for refusals for every party, the list of names, all 102 groups of remarks set aside, the 55 acts dated by their report, the changed text and the 55 answers. 40 rows are right as drawn and four needed a qualification; the principal counts and all 27 members' counts reproduce; all twelve acts the builder had added from the set-aside groups fit its four tests.
- The table of refusals was wrong in its split and too broad in its reading. It took the acts before the original sources found later were attached, so two lines of a transcript that the secretary of state's chip cites were counted outside: the published sources gave 54 lines inside and 77 outside, not 52 and 79. And it called the outside lines "articles" and gave only totals. The script now reads the published rows, counts lines and actor-article pairs, and lists every outside line with its classification (`research/nv8_refusals.tsv`): of 133 lines now matched, 57 stand in an article a refusal act of that actor cites; the other 76 (66 actor-article pairs) are 62 relays, each with the act it repeats, and 14 that are not refusals to comment. A relay is matched by what the page says was asked, which is not proof that no second request was made.
- Answers left beside refusals. Singapore's police: the chip had cut "While we do not enforce unilateral export controls imposed by other countries" from in front of "we act firmly"; it prints the whole sentence now. Taiwan's deputy foreign minister: that compliant travellers with no criminal record would not face unnecessary restrictions. Taiwan's China-policy agency: that it is not an intelligence agency, that visitors get routine screening unless a lead says otherwise, and that it can neither confirm nor deny the allegations. The secretary of state: "I don't know anything about that part of it" (Nov 8) and, of the travel to China, "you'll have to ask the White House" (Nov 9). The Justice Department's chip of Nov 12 now also carries Reuters's report that the Manhattan U.S. attorney's office declined to comment on Bloomberg's report. Anthropic's chip said it declined "to Quartz"; the page does not say who asked, so the chip now says that Quartz reports the refusal, the same caution as on the FBI's chip.
- One refusal moved a day. Commerce's "would not immediately comment on the other allegations" was drawn from a page of Nov 12; a saved page of Nov 11 attributes the same line to Axios. The act now sits on Nov 11, names Axios and cites both pages.
- Among the lines the added names brought, two responses I had not drawn, and one more beside them: the export bureau's answer to the Times of Nov 7 on licences (it reviews all applications; $324 million of penalties in 2025; more than 90 new officers), Nvidia's answer in the same article ("it makes no sense for the Chinese military to depend on American technology"), and Megaspeed's reply to the Times of Nov 9 (an internal investigation; offers to cooperate with the U.S. government that it says went unanswered). All three sit on their report's day; the Times page of Nov 7 prints an update of Nov 8, which the screen notes. One line under a general label for China gave the Washington embassy's answer and is matched to the embassy now; a post by a spokesperson of China's embassy in India, which I had matched to the Foreign Ministry, is matched to no row (it relays the ministry's statement, which is drawn). The reading listed 85 more labels the list lacked; 81 distinct ones are added (a formal name, a short form, a misspelling, a joint label), and a city's mayor is left unmatched because the row is the national government.
- Twelve further parts of drawn statements that meet my own four tests (the reading's count; two are in the items above), and one more from a gauge's table (Huang's memo). Nvidia on Nov 5, to CNBC: "We're confident that if the U.S. government believes that any person is a threat to American industry, it would let us know". The board on Nov 8: it "prioritizes U.S. national and economic security, which is paramount to the interests of NVIDIA and its shareholders". The chairman's release: "The Board has full confidence in Colette and the leadership team". The interim chief's email: "I believe deeply in this company and in its future", and a company meeting on Nov 18. Huang's memo: "My family bears no responsibility for my choices. I do". Huang's denial by phone on Nov 9, as the president relates it, now also on Huang's row. The president on Nov 8: "I haven't heard he's done anything wrong. He dealt with somebody, but you know, so does everybody else". An investor's post: "but my guess is there was no bad intention", cut from the middle of the words the chip quoted. Rep. Mast on Nov 12: Congress is not ruling out "public hearings for other people or other things down road". Singapore's and the secretary of state's are above. Most of these are reassurances, by Nvidia's side and by an official; I had added demands and denials more readily than reassurances, and the reading's count by actor shows it.
- From its second sample of what no gauge sees (22 actor-days with census lines, for the twelve actors ranked 13 to 24): Rep. Jeffries on CBS on Nov 9 ("I haven't been fully briefed on that issue"), a separate occasion from his news conference that day; his demand of Nov 12 for a classified briefing; and Sen. Schumer's demand, in his speech of Nov 10, for answers on "Nvidia's influence campaign". All three are drawn. An analyst who is drawn and heads his own firm gave CNBC an interview on Nov 12 that no chip carried ("AMD is clearly the immediate beneficiary", but it "couldn't supply"); it is drawn by the same rule as the remarks of OpenAI's board chairman. That rule, stated: a chief or chairman of a company that is already drawn can supply an act on that company's row, and the chip says in what role he spoke; a chief quoted once does not get a row of his own.
- Taiwan's government: three more. The head of its China-policy agency told lawmakers on Nov 12 how often she had entered (three times in 2024, five in 2025, none this year) and that she was not specially flagged; its science minister called it "an isolated incident" (Taiwan News, on the report's day); and the interior minister's chip of Nov 11 now carries her undertaking that "the police administration will provide full assistance".
- The text. The status had said every count of the sixteenth reading reproduced; 54 of its 56 recounts agreed, and it says so now. The note states the refusal sentence as a rule and says where its instances are listed, instead of asserting that every refusal is drawn. The note counts acts, not chips, whose passage may be newer than their day (eight now, some for one passage of several). The earlier list's sentence on what the added names "brought" now says what the builder drew from them.
- Where this stands. Seventeen readings in, each has still found parts of statements that no chip carries, and this one found as many as I had added. The four tests are a line that two careful readers draw in slightly different places, and the gauges read the readers' lines, not the pages. Left as limits: nine of the reading's 31 sample days have no dated line in the census and could not be tested; the list of names is kept by hand; whether one live page saved by hand as PDF held older entries stays open. The figure draws 248 dated acts, 60 of them on the day of their report. Nothing in this list has been read independently.

A reading by another company's model of the answer to the seventeenth reading, from a frozen copy of the public state at commit `a6b1c82` (the tables and figure of `50b1e9c`). For this reading each finding carries a mark, and the report counts distinct problems by mark: two errors in a row against its source, three separate occasions on no chip, fourteen further parts of statements already drawn, five faults in a table or the text, and one in a gauge.

What it confirmed: 24 of the 27 new or changed rows as drawn; the table of refusals line by line, with its counts; the 81 added names; that Anthropic's refusal is drawn by the same rule and with the same caution as the others; all 27 members' counts; and, on the interactive map, every act, tie, member line and listed piece against its public row.

Two wrong counts in the status, and one sentence of the README that claimed more than its table shows. And it says the four tests do not, as written, lead two careful readers to one list, and proposes a stricter procedure. Its remainder is the fourteenth reading's: whether one live page saved by hand as PDF held older entries.

**Changed in answer to the eighteenth reading, and what three more independent readers then found.** These changes were then read by the nineteenth reading, below. The list stands as it was released, except for three sentences that reading found wrong, which are corrected where they stand and marked:

- What the reading read. The answer to the seventeenth reading: 27 new or changed rows, the table of refusals line by line, the 81 added names, the changed gauge readings, a count of the last two rounds' additions by the direction their words cut, a third sample of what no gauge sees, twenty chips read against their whole statements, the 60 acts dated by their report, the changed text and the 178 answers. 24 rows are right as drawn; the refusal table's counts reproduce; all 27 members' counts are unchanged.
- Two rows were wrong against their source. Singapore's police: I had called my excerpt the whole sentence, and it stopped before its end; the chip now prints "we act firmly against companies and individuals who violate our laws and are engaged in fraudulent and dishonest practices", and the reply's next point, that Singapore has charged four individuals and four companies for fraud on suppliers of servers. Commerce's refusal of Nov 11: the chip said "two outlets"; it names Implicator and CryptoNews.
- Three separate occasions were on no chip, all posts by drawn commentators: one quoted in a newsletter of Nov 10 (a writer's allegation that the company's statements were false, with his doubt about what its spokesman believed), an analyst's post of Nov 11 ("This one will be hard, if not impossible, to recover from") and a broadcaster's post of Nov 12. Each is drawn, the first and third on their report's day.
- Fourteen further parts of statements already drawn, each added to its chip: the continuity sentence of the chairman's release (I had added half of what the seventeenth reader asked for); the board's committee of independent directors; Huang's "I am fully committed to NVIDIA and will work to earn back your confidence"; Sen. Warren's call for legislation on Oct 29; the Nov 17 deadline and the request for a briefing in the two senators' letter; the records the Wyden-Neal letter asks to be preserved; the dates in the three chairmen's letter (on each chairman's chip); Sen. Schumer's call for an immediate briefing of the intelligence committees; Rep. Himes's answers on what Congress was told and when; a newsletter writer's "I would be very surprised if Jensen Huang knew", his mitigating factor and his call on officials to resign; a broadcaster's investing guidance.
- Pages changed later. A relay of Nov 7 (the Jerusalem Post) carries Nvidia's answer to the Times whole and Commerce's without the word "all". The screen's two readings are corrected, and the note's number of acts that may be newer is recounted by script.
- Text. The status said "Sixteen" readings where it described seventeen, and "four refusals" where it listed five exchanges; both are corrected. One sentence of the list under the seventeenth reading was too strong: I wrote that its count by actor shows I had added demands and denials more readily than reassurances. The count has no denominator of all eligible statements. What the eighteenth reading's coding supports is narrower: of the twelve parts I added from the set-aside remarks six favoured Huang or Nvidia, and of the twelve the seventeenth reader then asked for, eight did.
- Whether the four tests are a line. The reading says they do not lead two careful readers to the same list, and proposes a stricter procedure: split each statement into its propositions, carry every answer about the actor's own conduct or knowledge, every denial, condition or limit that changes a printed proposition and every announced or requested action with its date, and write down why each remaining proposition is left out. I have not rebuilt the figure that way; the question is with the figure's owner.
- What the gauges do not see, read by three more independent readers (`research/nv8_single_article_remarks.tsv`, `research/nv8_paraphrased_acts.tsv`, `scripts/as_built/nv8_reading_lanes.py`). The gauges list a quoted remark only when two articles carry it, and compare words, so they miss what articles paraphrase. One reader took all 294 groups of quoted remarks by drawn actors that a single article gives and no chip carried, with 7 planted controls; two took the 2,027 paraphrased lines of Nvidia and of its board, chairman and interim chief, sorted them into 145 acts, and said which no chip carried, with four acts held out as controls. The first reader called all seven of its controls missing; the other two called three of their four missing and set the fourth aside as outside the days, because it is a report of these days about a call made in 2025 and their brief told them to leave earlier acts out. One of the two closed "reading: incomplete", for two first-publication days it could not settle; for the one that mattered, a reply to the Journal, the chip sits on Oct 31: the hand-saved copy of the article says it was updated that day, and a licensed copy of an earlier version, timed Oct 30, does not hold the sentence (this sentence first said only "I used the day of the hand-saved copy", which is not a reason; corrected after the nineteenth reading). They proposed 142 additions (rows of their tables; several often make one act). I checked each against its saved page: 125 are drawn and 17 are not, each with its reason in the tables (summaries of the company's standing position that name no occasion; a statement the page says was made "at the time" of an earlier claim; a translated account; relays of acts already drawn).
- What those readers found, by kind. Replies of Nvidia to single outlets that I had not drawn: to Tom's Guide on Oct 29, to the Journal on Oct 31 and Nov 10, to the FT on Nov 10, to the Daily Caller on Nov 11, to a nonprofit on Nov 12; the FT's report of what the general counsel said inside the company; reports of Nov 11 on the board's search and its deliberations; a later statement of Megaspeed's in the first Times article.
