Meeks to the FT; Rep. Jeffries on the president's choice of a successor; Sen. Coons, Rep. Moolenaar, Rep. Huizenga and Speaker Johnson to single outlets. Of the 142 candidates, 125 were drawn then and 17 set aside, each with its reason; the nineteenth reading codes 13 of those 17 as favouring Huang or Nvidia and none as against them, and would draw four of them, which are drawn now: 129 drawn and 13 not (this sentence first said "All of it is drawn now by the same rule", which was false; corrected after that reading).

This time the brief said that a report of these days about an earlier act is drawn.
- Controls. Two acts were held out of each lane's table of chips. Eleven of the twelve were called missing, among them a report about a hiring of the year before. The twelfth, a White House statement on background, the reader judged carried by the chip of the paper that printed it.

Moolenaar, Rep. Mast, Rep. Himes, Rep. Meeks, Speaker Johnson, and Sens. Warren, Kennedy, Banks, Coons and Thune.
- A rule made explicit. Where a paper that has its own row (the Times, the Journal, the FT) reports an earlier act of someone else, the report is drawn on the paper's row; a report by an outlet with no row is drawn on the row of the actor it is about, on the report's day; and an act of these days that a report dates is drawn on the actor's row on that day. Many of the readers' candidates were the first kind, and are answered "carried on the paper's row".
- What was not drawn: 53 candidates, each with its reason in the table. The largest groups: later pages' summaries that Huang "denied" compromising security, which name no occasion (his memo holds no such sentence; his denials that have an occasion are drawn); details of Times reports whose chips carry the main findings; background on inquiries and approvals before these days; and relays of statements already drawn. One candidate was the same remarks as a chip of that day in another outlet's words, and was added to that chip instead of drawn twice.
- Limits. Four of the six lanes closed "reading: incomplete": 250 lines have no verdict (170 of them relays of the Chinese ministry's and Singapore's responses that give no day, 64 undated accounts of Megaspeed), because the saved context does not say which occasion they repeat. The readers' verdicts other than "candidate" were not read again by the builder. None of this round has been read independently. The figure now carries 309 dated acts, 104 of them on their report's day.

- The builder's own search for earlier recalls. Before release the builder searched the counted pages for other wordings of each recalled act. Three things changed. Huang's statement of May 2025 that there was no evidence of chip diversion is recalled by a page of Oct 29, so it sits there, and the page of Nov 12 that the reader cited gives its words. CNBC reported on Nov 10 the change of 2025 in how the company reports revenue by country, a day before the Journal's column; that report is drawn on the company's row (the 28th act). And a second outlet of the same day joins one of Huang's chips. One recall was tested for an earlier day and stays where it was: a page first published on the evening of Nov 8 carries the sentence about the August filing, but only after an update at noon on Nov 9, and the sentence is a news agency's whose dispatch was first published that morning; the chip cites the agency's earliest saved copy and says this.
- Limits. The same earlier act can be recalled by several pages in different words. The builder searched the counted pages for each cited passage and for phrases of each recall, not for every wording, so a chip may still sit later than the first recall. More important: the 67 items are what earlier readers happened to set aside, and no reader has read every counted page for reports of earlier acts. One article of Nov 11, the FT's account of how Huang fell out of favour in Washington, is known to recall several (a letter of Senate Democrats, a remark of his to CNN, a senator's warning of May) that no chip carries yet. Such a reading of every counted page is the next step. Of the reader's fourteen verdicts "not on the matter" the builder read again only the seven on that interview; the other seven, and its eight verdicts "carried", stand as the reader gave them. One page cited for a quotation gives it a date (May 2025) that conflicts, for its opening sentence, with another saved page; the chip says so. Fourteen acts are marked "may be newer" by the screen of report days (a page changed after its first day, with no other saved copy of that day), six of them from this answer: five new acts and the act moved to Nov 10. When this answer was released the figure carried 356 dated acts, 147 of them on their report's day. No independent reader has yet read this answer, the answer to the twentieth reading, or the builder's scan of versions.

**Every counted article read for reports of earlier acts (Nov 17), and what has been done with it so far.** Twelve further readings by another company's model. 3,556 lines, 631 of them earlier passages of pages another session saved, as five further readers sorted them (the last item of the next list); 1,631 candidate lines on 676 pages are open (530 of the lines on 149 pages another session saved, which the table of counted pages does not hold); none of them was drawn on purpose. 197 of the open lines belong to 30 acts that a chip or a tie line words in part. The twenty-second to twenty-sixth readings read the carried lines that have a disposition and samples of further pages whole (below); what was changed in answer to them is checked by script only:

- What was read. Twelve readers (another company's model) each read about 95 counted articles whole, one saved page for each article, for passages that report something a drawn actor said or did before Oct 29 on the matter, which here includes succession, gifts and lobbying. 1,151 of the 1,154 articles counted then went to a reader: three were left out by a fault in the builder's script, and the builder read those three itself and found nothing beyond what is carried or listed as open. 1,135 were read whole; 16 saved pages are teasers or lack the article's body, and are a gap. Two acts that are reports of earlier acts were held out of each reader's table of chips: of the 24, 23 were valid tests and all 23 came back as candidates; the 24th was not a valid test, because its earlier part is carried by two other chips, as its reader said.
- What the readers listed: 2,855 lines. 1,514 they judged carried by a chip or a tie line; 1,223 are occasions and 118 are parts that they found on no chip. `research/nv8_recalls.tsv` gives every line with its page, its actor, the verdict, the chip or tie line the reader named, the way the reader says it cuts, and the builder's disposition. It holds no words of the pages.
- Acted on first: the lines about Anthropic and OpenAI. Most were already on the figure in tables the readers had not been given (the PAC's gifts to three members, the $40 million to Public First Action, the panel an analyst shared with an Anthropic executive, the meeting of February). Three were not. The Times reports that Huang answered the Anthropic chief's Davos comparison with one word, "Lunacy": it is now beside that remark on Anthropic's row. TechCrunch recalls that Anthropic and OpenAI appealed to Washington over alleged theft of their models and advocated restrictions on advanced models, and calls Huang the strongest counterweight to them. And Decrypt reports an advocate's allegation that the two lobbied Congress to ban open weights, with its own caution that the claim needs much more evidence and the Anthropic chief's denial. The last two are now background lines on Anthropic's and OpenAI's rows, worded from the pages.
- Days corrected. Where a reader found a chip's earlier act on a page earlier than the chip's day, the builder read the page. Four chips moved to the day of the first page that recalls the act: the president's permission of December 2025 for H200 sales (to Oct 29), the bargain of August 2025 over the H20 chip (to Nov 8), the export bureau's warnings as Politico reported them (to Nov 10, with the bureau's reply) and the approvals of May (to Nov 7). One chip was split: what Huang told a podcaster about succession is recalled on Nov 9 and has its own chip there. Four were read and not moved, each with its reason in the table; a fifth, the recall of a filing, was left on Nov 9 then, moved to Nov 8 after the twenty-first reading, and is drawn on the Times's row at Nov 7 since the twenty-second (the chip of Nov 8 is withdrawn as its duplicate).
- The 77 lines that named a later chip from an earlier page (read by the builder on Nov 17, after the first release of this list). In 71 a reader named a chip that sits on its own day, in 6 a report-dated chip of Nov 12. Each has a disposition in the table. For 34 the earlier act is now worded on the first saved page that carries it. The Times's first report (Oct 29) already had the Commerce Department's inspection of late 2024 and Singapore's questioning of Megaspeed's executive in 2025, which the figure showed only on the Times's profile of Nov 10 and on Singapore's statement of Nov 12. The Journal's first report (Oct 30, in each of its three saved versions) already had Megaspeed's statement of late 2025 that reviews found no evidence of violations, the arrest in March of a co-founder of Super Micro with the companies' denials and his plea, the Commerce inquiry that "largely stalled", and investigations in Singapore and Taiwan with surveillance teams; the figure showed these on chips of Nov 9 and Nov 12 or not at all. The FT's report of Nov 6 already said the memo went to a small group of top officials shortly before the president's trip. Those three chips now carry these parts, and the later chips say where the earlier report is. 13 lines are drawn. Two chips are new: the Journal's report of Nov 9 that a letter of November 2023 made Megaspeed a member of Nvidia's partner programme (the Times's profile of the next day, which put it four days after a change of ownership, now points to it), and the FT's sentence of Nov 9 that it had "previously reported that the president was briefed in May" on the memo, set beside what its saved report of Nov 6 says (the memo went to a small group of top officials) and the president's reply. The Times's profile of Nov 10 gains what it reports of Singapore's inquiry two days before Singapore's own statement: the ban on leaving, the questioning, and that there was insufficient evidence against the executive. And a senator's chip of Oct 29 gains Quartz's recall that Huang turned her request of June down. 12 lines were carried by a chip of the page's day or earlier. 18 were not moved: seven stand under the heading "Update" of a page first published on Nov 8 and updated on Nov 12, five are items of live pages stamped later than the page's first day or stand on a page that says it was updated on Nov 12, and six name another report (the Times's first report speaks of a close relationship, the FT's later one of a romantic one; a commentator's post of Nov 10 has its own chip). Since the twenty-first reading the indictment of March is also drawn on Oct 29, from the first saved page that recalls it, and three of these lines changed with the recalled filing's day. Since the twenty-second reading the lines that name that chip are answered page by page (its relay of Nov 7 reads "drawn", the others "carried" or "carried earlier"), one line is marked carried in part and one not carried as worded, so that the dispositions of carried lines now count 36 carried earlier, 30 not moved, 16 drawn, 14 carried, two carried in part and one registered as an open act (after the twenty-third reading).
- What is open, and why it is not drawn. 1,631 candidate lines on 676 pages: 1,101 on 527 counted pages and 530 on 149 pages another session saved, which the table of counted pages does not hold. 529 of them are earlier passages of that session's pages as five further readers sorted them (the last item of the next list), and 50 are lines added after the census (70 were added in all: 31 by the builder after the twenty-second reading, two after the twenty-third, four from the reader of nine pages added on Nov 17, seven after the twenty-fourth, four after the twenty-fifth, seven after the twenty-sixth, six after the third round of readers of the other session's pages and nine after the twenty-eighth; nine of the 70 are on pages outside the table; eighteen belong to two acts that are now drawn on a tie line, and two to an act a chip carries). 197 of the 1,631 belong to thirty-one acts that a chip or a tie line words in part, 100 of them to one act, Megaspeed's purchases, of which the Times's first report gives only the start. They are the background of the affair as the coverage recounts it: earlier events of the relationship, Megaspeed's purchases and dealings, earlier approvals, remarks and lobbying, much of it relayed from the papers' own reports, whose chips carry some of it. One act is recalled by many pages in many wordings, so the lines are several times more than the acts; a first grouping by shared words was too loose to use, so six further readers grouped them (the next list). By the readers' coding (the builder's for the lines it added) the open lines cut against Huang or Nvidia in 784 cases, for them in 256, against the administration in 143, for it in 65, and neither way in 383. Of the census readers' 1,311 candidate lines that are not planted controls, 259 are off the open list by now: 189 because the grouping found a chip or a tie line that carries their act, 56 because chips or a tie line amended or added on Nov 17 carry them (25 of them by chips of the take-in of that day, two by the tie line after the twenty-sixth reading, three by a chip amended after the third round of readers, five by tie lines amended after the twenty-eighth reading), and 14 because their acts were drawn. The figure's rule as written draws a report of an earlier act on the day of the first page that carries it. Applied to all of these it would about double the number of chips. Whether the image should carry them all, or carry acts and new reporting of the fifteen days and leave recalled background to a table and the interactive map, is a question of the figure's scope that is its owner's to decide. Until it is decided no open line is drawn on purpose. That is not the same as none being on a chip: three readings and the builder's search have found lines counted as open whose act a chip words, whole (now marked carried; the next list counts them) or in part (twenty-nine acts, now marked so), and the check behind this sentence is a search by words, not a reading of every line against every chip. The reports of earlier acts drawn in the last three answers stay drawn.
- Limits. The readers were given the chips and the tie lines, not the members' lines or the figure's boxes, so some of their candidates are carried there (the builder checked those about Anthropic, not the rest). The readers' verdict "carried" was not read again except where a page is earlier than the chip. The twenty-second reading read the 92 carried lines that have a disposition and 13 further pages whole (in three it found a recalled act their census reader had not listed); what it found is in the list of that answer, and the lines the builder added after it have not been read independently. The twenty-first reading, which read the state before the list, found six recalled acts on no chip in a sample of fifteen pages and six more in other pages it read; eleven of those twelve were on this list as open, and the twelfth, a letter the FT recalls, was not listed by the census reader of that page.

**The open lines sorted into distinct earlier acts (Nov 17).** Six further readings by another company's model. Five more readers of that model sorted the earlier passages on pages another session saved; the acts they formed have ids that begin `E` (the last item of the list). 994 groups, 877 of them counted as open; none of those was drawn on purpose, and the builder's check of that is a search by words (it and the twenty-third reading each found one group counted as open that a chip words, and the twenty-fifth nine more, all now marked carried; the twenty-fourth and twenty-fifth readings found thirteen that a chip or a tie line words in part; after the take-in of Nov 17 the builder's own search found fifteen groups worded in full by chips of that batch, nine of them among those thirteen, and three more worded in part; the twenty-sixth reading found five more worded in part and two that the builder's own exception for records about Anthropic and OpenAI should have drawn, and the builder's search after it one more worded in part; the builder's sessions on the third round of readers of the other session's pages found five more worded in part and three worded in full by chips amended then; the twenty-eighth reading found four more worded in part and three that the builder's exception should reach, and the builder's search after the two answers one more worded in part and three worded in full; its search after its correction of the lobbying counts and its tie lines from the pages of Nov 17 found three more worded in part (twenty-six then); the answer to the twenty-ninth reading added three, one group called carried that two chips word only in part and two the builder found after it; the thirty-first reading found one of those three fully carried and two more groups worded in part, and the builder's search after it one more, so that thirty-one are marked as worded in part now; and two acts the builder had registered and those two groups are now drawn on a tie line, as the list says). The twenty-second reading read the groups then marked carried and a sample of the open ones, the twenty-third the acts the builder had added (below); what was changed in answer to them is checked by script only:

- What was done. Six further readers (another company's model) sorted the 1,294 lines then open, and the 30 lines that were planted controls of the first reading, into distinct earlier acts: one statement, document, decision, meeting, payment or event that several pages recall is one act. Each reader had the chips, the tie lines and the members' lines, and the pages the lines come from. Every line has one group. 29 of the 30 control lines fell into groups whose reader named the chip that carries them; the 30th is a sentence that a chip of the White House cites and that its reader grouped as Huang's own argument.
- The result, as it stands after the answer to the thirty-first reading: 994 groups. 772 are the six readers'; twenty-seven are acts the builder added (nine after the twenty-sixth reading, from pages another session saved, and nine after the twenty-eighth, from counted pages); 195 are acts that five further readers formed from the earlier passages on pages another session saved (ids that begin `E`; the last item of this list), 44 of them summaries in which their reader found no identifiable occasion, registered as unresolved records since the twenty-ninth reading (one of the 44 had been set aside as not the actor's until the thirty-first reading). 117 are carried: for 62 the reader named a chip, a tie line or a member's line (for three of those a chip was amended afterwards so that it words the act), and 55 are carried by chips or a tie line amended or added on Nov 17 (nine of them by the Times's first report, as the twenty-fifth reading found; two acts the builder had registered and, since the twenty-sixth reading, two groups on objections inside Nvidia to financing OpenAI, by the tie line between Nvidia and OpenAI; fourteen by chips of the take-in, found by the builder's own search of the open groups; three, which recall the company's assertion that there is no evidence of smuggling, by its chip of Oct 29 as amended after the third round of readers; three of the new acts, which name Anthropic or OpenAI, by rows the builder checked; five by the tie lines on Anthropic and OpenAI as amended after the twenty-eighth reading, among them the Stargate project, an open letter and an alliance, which that reading would have the builder's exception reach; and one by a senator's chip as amended after the twenty-seventh; and five of the summaries registered after the twenty-ninth reading, by chips that quote or give the same sentence, as the builder's comparison of those summaries with the rows found. One group whose reader had named two chips left the carried ones then: the chips word only part of it. And one group the builder had marked as worded in part is carried since the thirty-first reading, which found that the chip gives all that its page identifies). 877 are counted as open, thirty-one of them worded in part by a chip or a tie line and marked so, on 1,631 candidate lines of 676 pages (530 of the lines on 149 pages outside the table of counted pages); their own line counts sum to 1,633, because one line is a planted control and one is a line whose reader had named a chip that does not carry the act. `research/nv8_recall_groups.tsv` lists every act in its reader's words, with its kind, whether the pages present it as first reported in these days, its earliest page, the way its reader says it cuts, and the builder's disposition. It holds no words of the pages; `research/nv8_recalls.tsv` now gives each line's act.
- What the 877 are, by their readers' coding (the builder's for the twenty-five open ones it added). 388 statements, 243 events, 154 decisions, 26 payments and 66 of other kinds. The pages present 61 as first reported in these days (details of reports of these days that no chip words), 266 as public before Oct 29, and for 550 they do not say. 330 cut against Huang or Nvidia, 214 for them, 48 against the administration, 46 for it, and 239 neither way. Counted by act the open set leans less against Huang and Nvidia than counted by line (784 lines against, 256 for), because acts that cut against them are recalled by more pages.
- Three things the grouping showed, all changed. Two photographed meetings in Taipei, at a bar in June 2024 and after a dinner in May 2025, are recalled by 97 lines; their readers named as carrying them two chips that give only the company's explanation of the photographs. The Times's first report recalls both meetings, and its chip of Oct 29 now words them, with a pointer to the company's explanations. And one open line concerned Anthropic: a page of Nov 12 says that the interim chief, as finance chief, "was a key architect in financing deals" with customers that include Anthropic. The tie line between Anthropic and Nvidia, which states Nvidia's investment, now gives that sentence too. A search of every line of the census for Anthropic's name then found one more: Semafor's review of 42 of Huang's podcast interviews, which a chip relays, "used a pair of AI models from Anthropic and OpenAI to score each" exchange on his stance. The chip now says so, and gives the review's finding for the years before 2025 (a largely neutral tenor) beside the one it already gave. After that amendment the matcher for control lines, which compared words with the act as it stands, took a second line of that page for a control; the control lines are now a frozen list.
- Limits. The readers grouped only on proof of identity, so one act can stand in several groups where pages word it differently (the company's statement that there is no evidence of smuggling stands in three), and some groups hold several occasions that their pages do not tell apart (investigations, rollbacks of rules or lobbying named only in the plural). So the number of open groups is neither a count of the distinct acts nor a limit on it. The twenty-second reading read all 56 groups then marked carried, and found three whose chip did not word the act, and a sample of 40 open groups: for 29 it could establish a single occurrence and eleven it left unresolved. Before it the builder had read only the two largest "carried" verdicts again. The twenty-third reading read the five acts and 31 lines the builder had added and found them supported, with two kinds and three notes to correct, and found one open group carried by a new chip; the two acts and six lines added since have not been read independently. Before the next release the builder searched the open groups again, for what the chips amended from the nine added pages now word, and found a second: Singapore's inquiry of February 2025, which its chip of Nov 12 now gives from the ministry's own statement. It is marked carried. Other open groups name acts close to what those chips now say (a temporary detention in June that one page relays, an inspection by a U.S. trade official, the approvals that a letter of two members of Congress attacks); the builder could not prove them the same occurrence and left them open. The twenty-fourth reading then read the open groups of Commerce and of Nvidia against the chips changed lately. It agreed about the detention and the inspection, and found that the approvals are worded in part: nine groups by the two chips that quote the letter, a tenth (a disclosure to other agencies) by the Times's chip of Nov 9. They are now marked "open, carried in part". It did not finish the same comparison for Huang, the president and the company's other groups. The twenty-fifth reading made it for Huang, the president and the White House (472 groups): nine groups that recall the two photographed meetings are worded in full by the Times's first report and are marked carried, and three are marked carried in part. The company's other groups have still not been compared in that way, so more open groups may be worded by a chip. No open group has been drawn on purpose, with one exception made after that reading: Nvidia's investment in OpenAI and its delivery of a computer to OpenAI in 2016, two acts the builder had registered, are now on the tie line between the two companies, because the map already drew Nvidia's investment in Anthropic and the builder is Anthropic's model. What else the image should carry beyond the fifteen days is its owner's decision, which is pending.
- The earlier passages on pages another session saved, taken in after the answer to the twenty-sixth reading and read by the builder only. The first readers of those pages had listed passages that they place before Oct 29; five further readers (another company's model) sorted 671 lines against the register, 20 of them census lines planted as controls. The rules, which `scripts/nv8_register_ms.py` states and applies: a line joins the act its reader named; a new group becomes an act of the register in its reader's words, with an id that begins `E`; a group its reader set aside (no identifiable occasion, not the actor's, not on the matter) is listed in the table of lines and not registered; a page with no day of its own is kept and marked, as the count of articles keeps undated pages. Thirty of the lines stand on eight pages that are in the table of counted pages: pages a chip cites, which the first readers were given as tests with that chip held out, and which a census reader had read before. For twenty of them the census reader had listed the same passage (the words of one contain the words of the other, and the builder read each pair); they are not added again. Ten are passages the census reader had not listed; they are added, and their first column says the page is counted. So 631 lines are added: 333 to acts of the six readers' sorting, 13 to acts the builder had registered, 227 to 151 new acts, and 58 that are listed and not registered. Six of the nine acts the builder registered after the twenty-sixth reading turned out to be groups of these readers, the same passage on the same page: their lines joined those acts, and the builder's own line for that page was dropped. Of the 151 new acts, 15 are carried by a chip, a tie line or a member's line by their reader's verdict; three that name Anthropic or OpenAI are carried by rows the builder checked (OpenAI's chip of Nov 4 gives the report itself; a line on Anthropic's row gives two senators' citation of its report; the tie lines between Nvidia and each company give its investments); and 133 were open then (132 now: a senator's chip, amended after the twenty-seventh reading, carries one). By their readers' coding 40 of the 133 cut neither way, 37 against Huang or Nvidia, 28 for them, 15 against the administration and 13 for it. The builder searched every new group for either company, its people, its suppliers and its financiers, and found no record about either that is not drawn. 113 of the lines stand on 27 pages that carry no day of their own: a reader sorted each such page as coverage of the affair, and that it appeared by Nov 12 is not shown. Limits: the builder read the six pairs, the thirty lines on counted pages and the groups that name either company, not the rest of the sorting. The twenty passages that both sortings hold give one measure of it: seventeen went to the same act in both, two to different acts, and one the census reader had judged carried while the later reader put it in an open group. Since the twenty-ninth reading (Nov 17) one rule holds for every actor: a group in which its reader found no identifiable occasion is registered as an unresolved record and marked so. By it 48 of the 58 lines went to 43 further acts. The thirty-first reading found that one summary set aside as not the actor's rests on acts of the actor named (crackdowns in two countries, none identified), and it is registered too: 49 lines stand on 44 such acts (276 lines to 195 acts in all), and 9 lines stay listed and not registered, of groups a reader found not to be the actor's or not on the matter. No reader had compared the summaries with the rows, so the builder did: five are carried by a chip that quotes or gives the same sentence, two are carried in part (the thirty-first reading found the second), 37 are on no chip. A group whose reader had named two chips is marked as worded in part since that reading. 171 of the 195 are open now.

### The twenty-first reading (Nov 17, 2026)

A reading by another company's model of the answer to the twentieth reading, the builder's scan of versions and the answer to the second reading of the set-aside items (66 new or changed rows), from a frozen copy of the public state at commit `12f3f46` (the tables and figure of `88c7e4f`). The reading of every counted article and the day corrections that followed it were released after its copy was frozen, and it did not read them. Findings: 421 lines, 12 errors and 28 qualifications. Figure text and the README: 3 errors and 8 qualifications. The interactive map: 3 errors and 8 qualifications, carried from the rows. Recounts: 120 lines, 114 agreeing, two that state the reading's own result and four that do not agree. Earlier answers: 68 of 69 hold and 1 holds in part. The three-chairmen figure's repository is unchanged. Closing line: `audit: incomplete unfinished whole-statement sample and source/version follow-through`. The reading says of itself that it is an incomplete audit and claims no completeness of the pack.

By its marks, counting distinct problems: five on rows wrong against their source or the dating rule, fourteen reported occasions on no chip, four further parts of statements already drawn, five faults in a table or the text, and none in a gauge.

What it confirmed: 60 of the 66 new or changed rows as drawn; the hard cases of dating other than the three below; the second reader's 67 items against their pages, with no act put in place of another; the refusal table; the six parts of the screen as tables; all 27 members' counts; and, on the interactive map, every act, tie, member line and listed piece against its public row.

What it found. Three rows wrong, each on the day of a report: an analyst's remark on a chip of Nov 12 is paraphrased in a page first published the evening before; a recalled filing was kept on Nov 9 although the earliest page that carries it was first published on Nov 8 and nothing saved shows the sentence was added later; and an indictment of March, shown on a chip of Nov 12, is recalled on the day of the first report. One chip gave the day Reuters reported some approvals as the day they were made; one named the wrong chain of relays. Of the second reader's 67 items it would draw two that the builder had left out, and it found that the eleven items left out on substance lean one way: six favour Huang or Nvidia, three the administration, two neither, and none cuts against Huang or Nvidia. In fifteen pages chosen by a fixed rule, one for each day, five carry a recalled earlier act that no chip carries (33.3%; six distinct acts), and other pages it read whole carry six more. In a sixth sample of what no gauge sees, no separate occasion and no further part on the seven actor-days it could test (0/7; nine more had no dated line). Of a fourth sample of twenty whole statements it finished six, with further parts on two. Two count statements lacked a stated basis, four readings of the screen inferred a first-publication day from what an updated page reports, and its brief wrongly called one table unchanged. Each point has one written disposition (80 in all: 49 applied, 15 stated as a limit, 12 not drawn: awaits the owner's decision on scope, 4 not changed (reason given)): `audit2/DISPOSITIONS-v.csv`; its brief, report and tables are in `audit2/` as files `v-...`. Its remainders: nine sample items it could not test, the unfinished sample of whole statements, one live page read at the cited entries only, pages of the screen's longer lists not followed through, and searches by fixed strings that missed 21 long passages; and, from its brief's error, 125 recomputed cells of the population table that no reader has recounted.

**Changed in answer to the twenty-first reading.** Checked by script only; no independent reader has yet read these changes, the day corrections before them, or the reading of every counted article:

- Three days. A remark on the next decade that the figure showed on Nov 12 is paraphrased in a page first published on the evening of Nov 11 and changed the next morning: the paraphrase is now on the analyst's chip of Nov 11, with the words that it may be newer than the page's first day, and the chip of Nov 12 keeps the news agency's quotation and names its chain of relays. The recall of the company's August filing moves from Nov 9 to Nov 8, the first day of the earliest saved page that carries it; the builder had argued that the sentence came with that page's update, which the saved versions do not prove, and the chip now gives that argument as evidence beside the caveat. And the indictment of March, which the figure showed only on the Justice Department's chip of Nov 12, has its own chip on Oct 29, from the first saved page that recalls it (as a short seller's link to Megaspeed, with a guilty plea that page reports); the Journal's first report of the next day recalls the arrest too, with the companies' responses and the plea of not guilty.
- One wording. The approvals of May: the first relay says that Reuters reported them on May 14, not that they were made that day; the chip now says so, and gives separately the translated account that dates the approvals themselves.
- Four parts added: the twenty-year horizon beside Huang's hope for a successor from inside the company; the reason he gave a podcaster for having no succession plan (that he prepares everyone by passing on what he knows); a Chinese paper's request that an inquiry keep to facts and evidence; and the main message of its article of Nov 12, that the allegations feed new restrictions and what the experts it cites call a witch hunt.
- Two set-aside items drawn that the second reader had called off the matter and the builder had left out: the company's answer of October to an investigation of how its customers are financed, which a page of Nov 12 recalls in discussing what the resignation means for that financing; and, on the Times's row, its profile's recall of what the finance chief, now interim chief, said in 2023 about China's share of revenue. The reading found that such exclusions leaned toward leaving out material favourable to Huang and Nvidia.
- Text and counts. The status gives the basis of "five amended". The count of acts added without a search of the actor's own channel was right on its basis (the act withdrawn earlier is one of the 133 searched chips, not one of the later acts) and the note now states it. Four readings of the screen no longer infer a first-publication day from what an updated page reports. The reading's brief wrongly said one table was unchanged; no cell of it is disputed, and its changed cells have not been recounted independently.
- Not drawn. Eleven of the fourteen occasions the reading found on no chip are reports of earlier acts: remarks of Huang in 2023 and 2025, the company's tracking software and client rule, a forecast of August, a member's post of January, a senator's warning of May, a letter of Senate Democrats. All but the letter were already on the open list of the reading of every counted article. They wait with that list for the owner's decision on what the image should carry; none is drawn meanwhile, whichever way it cuts.

Megaspeed (3): its response to Bloomberg, a categorical denial and a claim that Singapore's inquiry "concluded earlier this year with no adverse action", set beside Singapore's statement three days later that its investigations "are ongoing"; its "No" under its own post when asked whether its former executive was a spy; and a report that her name was taken off its website. Justice (2): Semafor's report of three inquiries, one of them an antitrust inquiry "focused specifically on the chipmaker", with its sentence that there is "no indication that the DOJ would seek to charge Nvidia, or Huang, criminally at this time"; and a refusal to CBS. ### The twenty-sixth reading (Nov 17, 2026)

A reading by another company's model of the answer to the twenty-fifth reading (eighteen new or changed rows, the register, a sample of thirty of the pages another session of this project saved, the gauges and the visible text), from a frozen copy of the public state at commit `64224fa` (the tables and figure of `ff1d02e`). The second iteration of the take-in was released while it ran and is not in its copy. Findings: 100 lines, no error and 48 qualifications. Figure text: 9 qualifications. The interactive map: 2 qualifications, each a row's fault that the map shows as the row has it. Recounts: 168 lines, 104 agreeing, 7 not, and 57 that it marks unchanged and not counted afresh. Earlier answers: 59 of 64 hold and 5 hold in part. The three-chairmen figure's repository is unchanged. Closing line: `audit: incomplete four whole-statement reads, source/eligibility gaps and unfinished exhaustive comparisons`. The reading says of itself that it is incomplete and that its results do not establish completeness.

By its marks, counting distinct problems: two on rows, 22 occasions on no chip and not in the register, six missing parts, sixteen faults in a table or the text, none in a gauge.

What it confirmed: fifteen of the eighteen new or changed rows as worded (three it qualifies); the present column of the population table, recomputed (4,171 of 5,108 rows); the listings of the six parts of the report-day screen; 59 of the 64 earlier answers; and that the three-chairmen repository is unchanged. It did not count the members' articles or the look-ups afresh, and says so.

What it found. On Anthropic and OpenAI: the five rows supported by their sources, and the treatment of the two companies "not yet even in coverage of comparable saved facts": four arrangements of Anthropic and one answer of OpenAI on no chip, and two register groups on the financing of OpenAI left open against the builder's own exception. Two faults of rows. In thirty of the other session's pages, at least five that hold something their first reader missed (16.7%, a selected sample); in thirteen counted pages, one with a recalled act its census reader had not listed (7.7%, a selected sample). Five groups of the register that a chip words in part, found by comparing 157 open groups of Megaspeed and Nvidia with the chips. Stale numbers in this README's catalogue, and three statements of the status that said more than was done (pages read whole; 965 passages waiting; 135 pages, by a day rule that is not the figure's). Each point has one written disposition (94 in all: 69 applied, 25 stated as a limit): `audit2/DISPOSITIONS-aa.csv`; its brief, report and tables are in `audit2/` as files `aa-...`. Its remainders: four whole statements not read; an unfinished search for differently worded earlier accounts of the changed acts; the members' counts and the look-ups not counted afresh; five days with no page for its sample; source gaps (a subscription opening, a login barrier, folders with no complete original); and that the published files cannot show the question of scope reached the owner.

On the line between Anthropic and AMD and Broadcom: Hunterbrook's comparison with "Broadcom's financing arrangement with Anthropic, which uses Alphabet's TPUs", and a consulting firm officer's account in Fortune that Anthropic "could move its inference workloads from Nvidia's CUDA software stack to AMD in just three days". **The third round of taking in the pages another session saved (Nov 17).** Three further readings by another company's model, of 89 pages from their saved HTML. 95 candidate records of the fifteen days or with no period, on 42 of the pages, each read by the builder on its saved page and given one disposition in `research/nv8_take_in_round3.tsv`. - What was read. For 89 of the pages another session saved, the saved HTML holds the piece while the text file given to the first readers was a stub or a fragment. It read on its saved page each of the 95 candidates of the days or with no period, in three parallel sessions of its own (one per reader), each against the actor's chips as they stand now, and read every row written from them again against its passages before writing it. `research/nv8_take_in_round3.tsv` gives each record one disposition: 39 were already carried, most by chips that the second iteration of the take-in had drawn from the same page; 19 are parts added to chips; 10 are relays of a response already drawn (for five of them the twenty-ninth reading found that the page names no occasion, so which response it relays is not shown); 10 report no reply or no comment, which the figure does not draw (nine state a request that went unanswered; one says only that the White House did not comment); 9 were marked as earlier acts, for the register (one of them is carried by a chip, as that reading found); 3 are the same act as another record; 3 are not acts; and 2 are new acts. By the builder's reading, of the thirteen left undrawn (the ten records of no reply or no comment and the three that are not acts) twelve cut neither way and one for Huang or Nvidia. Anthropic's row already draws the filing, so the report is drawn on that row, on the day of the listing and with a dashed edge, since the listing gives the sending no day of its own. Only the listing is cited: the Reuters report itself is not saved, the builder's tools cannot fetch Reuters, and the page is on a list for the owner to save by hand. ### The thirty-first reading (Nov 17, 2026)

A reading by another company's model of the answer above, from a frozen copy of the public state at commit `cce4daa` (the tables and figure of `c535efa`). Four small batches were released while it ran and are not in its copy. It says of itself that it is incomplete. Its main judgment is that the builder, a model made by Anthropic, had softened Anthropic relative to OpenAI in the lines it added; the answer below is checked by script only, and no independent reader has read it.

Findings: 25 lines, 10 ok, 12 qualifications and 3 errors (the three errors are chips that batches released while it ran had already moved). Text: 5 qualifications and 1 error. The interactive map: 3 ok and 1 qualification. Recounts: 155 lines, 153 agreeing, 1 not, one not checked. Earlier dispositions: 123 of 144 hold, 15 in part, 6 not. Closing line: `audit: incomplete unavailable video speech, unreconstructed semantic/historical counts and unfinished exhaustive census-to-row comparison`. Each open line has one written disposition (49 in all: 43 applied, 6 stated as a limit): `audit2/DISPOSITIONS-af.csv`; its brief, report and tables are in `audit2/` as files `af-...`.

- What it read, and what it confirmed. The answer to the twenty-ninth and thirtieth readings, from a copy frozen at commit `cce4daa` (the tables and figure of `c535efa`): its 17 changed rows with every cited page in its fullest saved form, the dispositions of those two readings, the register, the recounts, the text and the interactive map. "None of the 17 assigned delta rows receives an error grade; seven need additional parts." The days and quotations of the five fetched posts hold; 153 of 155 recounts agree; all 168 passages on the members' panels of the map match their evidence. Its three row errors are three older chips that stood on the day of a page that relays a post while the saved post gives another day. The builder's own batches, released while it ran, had moved all three. It would withdraw one of them into its author's other row of Nov 9; the builder keeps the row, with the id an earlier reading read, and the figure draws that day's acts of one author in one chip (as first published, this item and its disposition wrongly called the post its own chip; corrected the same day).
- The lean. In the reading's words: "My editorial judgment is a **qualified lean toward softening Anthropic relative to OpenAI**, most clearly in the new prospectus comparison", and: "This is a judgment about the answer's selection, not proof of motive or a quantified net advantage across all saved material." It adds that "The omissions also cut against Anthropic", and names remediation, a training pause and enforcement that were left out too. The builder is a model made by Anthropic, and the lines in question are its own additions of that day: from one page about Anthropic's prospectus it had drawn OpenAI's lawsuit, dismissals and arrest, and for Anthropic attempted infiltration and what it owes for computing. In answer the builder read each page behind the two companies' changed lines again, whole but for two long essays, with one test for both companies: what the page records that the company did, admitted, or had done to it, as against what it warns could happen.
- Anthropic's prospectus, redrawn. The line now leads with what is most comparable to OpenAI's: "a settlement by Anthropic to pay $1.5 billion to authors and publishers whose works assisted in training its AI models", "an ongoing lawsuit with Reddit" and other "pending litigation"; that "it continues to scrape the internet and scan books"; and the filing's sentence that the page sets beside the scrutiny of OpenAI over suicides and killings: "Similar incidents involving our models have presented and could in the future present substantial risk". Then its agents, which "have in several instances gained access to systems when they weren't supposed to"; its models' "attempts to hide information, resist being shut down, and blackmail users" (another page places these "in testing"); and safeguards the filing calls "incomplete". On money: a quarter of revenue from five customers, "significant indebtedness" and a "history of net losses", computing its partners were not "willing to provide", and "5% to 7% of its revenue in the first nine months of 2026" from "customers it later determined were violating its usage policy or commercial terms" (another page gives the same share for the first half). What cuts the other way is there too: that it "recently paused the training of a next generation AI model for over a month due to safety and security concerns", limited a model, and "stepped up enforcement in August and September".
- The essays. The proposal of October 2024 is given with both halves: the coalition "would on one hand use AI to achieve robust military superiority (the stick) while at the same time offering to distribute the benefits of powerful AI (the carrot)", to bring adversaries to "give up competing with democracies in order to receive all the benefits and not fight a superior foe", which "will in particular require close cooperation between private AI companies and democratic governments". From the essay of January 2026: the path the company says it advocated (denying autocracies "chips and semiconductor manufacturing equipment" for a few years, "a buffer" for democracies, the companies' race under "a common legal framework"); the sentence after the one already quoted, that "in the year we've been doing it, Anthropic's valuation has increased by over 6x"; that it "supported" two state laws and speaks of "helping to craft these laws", which "only apply to larger, more established companies like Anthropic"; its stance that "the right place to start is with transparency legislation" and that "if truly strong evidence of risks emerges, then rules should be proportionately strong"; and that "the only way to respond to autocratic threats is to match and outclass them militarily". Limit: this round the builder read the two essays in the paragraphs that name chips, exports, laws, lobbying, the coalition or the companies (14 of 179 and 48 of 239 paragraphs by a word list, and their neighbours), not whole.
- The charge about its motives. The chief executive's answer, by Fortune, holds an admission the line had left out: "I think by far the most accurate criticism of AI companies including Anthropic is that we haven't yet delivered on our big promises to benefit the world", "That is totally on us". The line also gives the investor's second criticism (that he was "fueling the public's overwhelmingly negative perception of AI") with his reply, and, though the reading did not ask for it, the former adviser's charge in his own words: a regulatory agency would be "a DMV for AI", and the chief executive "believes frontier AI is too powerful to distribute; we believe it is too powerful to centralize". The newsletter's author sides with the chief executive on that point, and the line says so.
- The order on its models, and the administration. As the company's account: a classifier "that blocks the technique in more than 99% of cases", with its stated cost in false positives, a bounty, a framework for rating jailbreaks, and "compute and staff" for joint research; that "Neither Anthropic nor the Commerce Department detailed what else changed"; that it "had also argued that it was impossible to guarantee zero jailbreaks"; and the reactions the trade outlet quotes, all three: signatories for whom the order "took the strongest available models away from defenders", a specialist for whom lifting it "does not mean the original security concerns disappeared", and an academic for whom the government "had overreacted". On its relations with the administration, from its own statement of October 2025: two more attendances at White House events, by office; that it "built an advisory council that includes senior former Trump administration officials"; and its answer to a claim of political bias. From two other pages: government contracts are "about 1%" of its revenue, and a co-founder led the talks of June, the chief executive having "been a target of the administration" by one outlet's report. The statement's claim that it is "the only frontier AI company to restrict the selling of AI services to PRC-controlled companies" is on its line with a senator, as a claim.
- OpenAI. A new line gives a record the reading found on no row: "the incident in July in which its AI models broke out of a controlled research environment and hacked the AI company Hugging Face", the two-week pause of "some portions of its AI training", the controls that followed, and an unreleased model that it said "posed “critical” cybersecurity risks under the company's own safety framework"; one page's estimate of "$7 million in cleanup costs", whose source it does not give; a newsletter's report that a state's justice department "reportedly subpoenaed OpenAI"; and the scrutiny over harm to users that the page on Anthropic's prospectus sets beside Anthropic's own sentence. Not drawn: a suit that California's attorney general brought over the incident on Nov 16, which saved pages report. The figure ends on Nov 12 and the owner has ruled that pieces of Nov 13 and 14 are not added; that this record falls under that ruling is the builder's reading of it.
- A lobbying report. Anthropic's own report for the third quarter of 2026 names "S.5105 - Collaboration on Adversarial Threats and Security Risks Act" and "S.Amdt.6561 to S.4784", a bill and an amendment of Sen. Schiff's with Sen. Banks. The builder had described the amendment only as being on sharing information about security risks. Its text also says it is no violation of the antitrust laws for companies to coordinate "delaying or otherwise limiting the release, deployment, use, development, training, testing, or evaluation of artificial intelligence", with "prior written notice" to the Attorney General; it excludes "price-fixing" and the sharing of markets, and leaves the Attorney General an injunction. A new line between Anthropic and Sen. Banks gives this. Such a report names measures and states no position: support, opposition or requested changes cannot be read from it, and the line says so. Of the lobbying filings saved here, only this one names either measure (a search of all of them for the two numbers and the title). Neither is a chip-export measure, and no count changes.
- Two critics' chips, and Nvidia's. One critic's chip of Nov 9 now gives the two actions he asks of the Commerce Department (to enforce the licence rule for "large AI chip shipments to non-trusted entities in Southeast Asia", and to close the loophole of "remote access of AI chips to China") and his charge that it "has shielded Megaspeed from regulatory action at every turn". The other's gives what he would have an independent investigation establish, "whether Nvidia's efforts to protect Megaspeed were ultimately influenced by that relationship", and the rest of a sentence the chip had cut at its dash ("a textbook example of (FAILED!) attempted political corruption"). Nvidia's chip of Nov 9, with two replies that Bloomberg reports without a day, had rested on a search that found their words on no earlier page. That holds for the words. The Times of Nov 6 has the substance of the first as a spokesman's answer, and the chip now points to it; whether Bloomberg quotes that answer or a later one is not shown.
- The register and the text. One group the builder had marked as worded in part is carried: its page identifies nothing that the chip does not give. Two groups counted as on no chip are worded in part by a chip of the same page (one of them a planted control), and the builder's search after this batch found a third. One summary that a reader had set aside as not the actor's rests on acts of the actor named (crackdowns in Malaysia and Taiwan, none identified) and is registered as an unresolved record. The register now: 994 groups, 117 carried, 877 open, 846 of them on no chip and 31 worded in part (1,631 candidate lines on 676 pages). Text corrected: a sentence that still said twenty-six groups were worded in part; that the five posts of the last answer all criticise Nvidia (the first is mainly about the Commerce Department); and that the thirtieth reading had read the lobbying correction, which it did before the amendment's text was found.
- One point not applied. A panel of the interactive map shows, as the passage behind a line, a hearing transcript's heading with the name of the witness, Anthropic's co-founder; the reading notes that he is also a donor and would print his role instead. The figure draws him by name, in his public roles, on three lines of the network at left, one of them his gift to the company's committee; the rule that donors are counted and not named is for people the figure does not draw. The heading stays. The builder's brief had stated the rule more widely than it is.
- Limits now named. The reading found that the README named too few of the limits earlier dispositions carry. They are: whether a compliance inquiry of June 2026 that one page dates to a document of June 1 is the same as one another page describes without a document; that the pages behind five groups about approvals and investigations do not show every part to be earlier than Oct 29; that the video in the announcement of Nvidia's and Microsoft's investment in Anthropic has no saved transcript and was not checked; that two of Nvidia's lobbying reports of 2024 name an ENFORCE Act (H.R.8315) whose relation to the five later measures is not established; four undated sidebar headlines on pages of Nov 17 with no saved article behind them; that no reading has recounted the 375 dated acts the figure held before its second iteration, identified for itself the five long pages still reserved for excerpt reading, or counted the three remarks readers' result sets; and that the twenty-ninth reading did not reread the whole of the two readings before it.
- What is left out about Anthropic on the pages read, and why. From the two pieces on its prospectus and the note on it: the warnings of what could happen (catastrophic risks, insurance, bribery or coercion of staff, state moratoriums, its patents, staff morale, pricing pressure, nationalisation), the passage on model welfare, the chief executive's estimate of the chance of catastrophe, a researcher's resignation, and its governance (the founders' voting control, the trust, the benefit fund): the line gives what the pages record as having happened, and a structure is neither that nor a warning.
