### A correction by the builder: lobbying reports that name a measure without a bill number (Nov 17, 2026)

Not a reading: the builder found this itself, in both companies' counts, after the release that answered the twenty-eighth reading. Checked by script only; no independent reader has read it.

- What was wrong. In the members' rows, the lines on lobbying reports counted a report for a member's bill only where the report gives a bill number. A report that named a measure as an amendment to another bill, or by its name alone, was in no count, for either company. Four reports name the GAIN AI Act that way: Anthropic's own report for the third quarter of 2025 (Senate amendment 3505 to the defense authorization bill for fiscal 2026, with the act's name), a firm's report for Anthropic for the fourth quarter of 2025 (the act's name alone), and Nvidia's own reports for the third and fourth quarters of 2025 ("S.A. 3505", without the act's name).
- How it was found, and a wrong first reading. The builder found it on Nov 17 while reading pages another session had saved from the Senate's lobbying records. Its first check searched both companies' reports for the names of the five measures, found Anthropic's two, and recorded that Nvidia had none. That record was right about the words and wrong about the thing: Nvidia's reports give the amendment's number and not the act's name. A second search, for every amendment number in both companies' reports, found them before anything was released. The amendment is identified from its own text as Congress.gov prints it (short title "GAIN AI Act of 2025"; submitted by Sen. Banks on Aug 1, 2025), saved from the Internet Archive because the live page refuses this machine.
- The rule now, one for both companies: a report counts for a measure when its text gives a bill number of the measure, or the number of a Senate amendment whose official text carries the measure, or the measure's short or long name. The three bases are counted apart in `research/lobbying_family_reports.csv`. The count by bill number is unchanged: `research/lobbying_bill_reports.csv` is the same file, byte for byte. GAIN AI Act: Anthropic 4 reports (2 by bill number, 1 by the amendment with the act's name, 1 by name alone); Nvidia 3 (1 by bill number, 2 by the amendment's number alone). For the other four measures (the AI OVERWATCH Act, the Chip Security Act, the MATCH Act, the Remote Access Security Act) no report of either company falls outside the count by bill number.
- What changed in the figure. Two members' lines and nothing else: Rep. Moolenaar's line on H.R. 5885 and Sen. Banks's on S. 3150 keep their counts by bill number (1 and 1; 1 and 0) and add that 2 more reports of each company name the act without a bill number. The fuller wording in `research/member_ties.csv` says how each report names it and that Sen. Banks submitted the amendment. No id changed. The three-chairmen figure, which shares the table, carries the same correction in Moolenaar's panel.
- Also corrected in the script: its window. It read every folder of saved reports whose name begins with the company's, and folders of 2024 reports, saved on Nov 13 for another table, would have joined its next run. The years 2025 and 2026 are now written in its code. None of the 22 saved reports of 2024 gives a bill number or a name of the five measures.
- Limits. One Anthropic report, for the third quarter of 2026, names Senate amendment 6561 to the defense authorization bill for fiscal 2027. Its text was found after this correction was first published, in the Congressional Record of July 13, 2026: an amendment by Sen. Schiff, for himself and Sen. Banks, that would exempt from antitrust law the sharing of information on security risks of artificial intelligence and agreed limits or delays on its development and release, with notice to the Attorney General (the builder had described it only as being on sharing information; a tie line between Anthropic and Sen. Banks gives its text since the thirty-first reading). It carries none of the five measures and is counted for none; the members' lines count chip-export measures only. An amendment is recognised only by its number: a report that describes a provision in its own words, with no name or number, is counted for no measure. The pages the other session saved from the Senate's records (searches of all filers' reports for the names of three of the measures, and two other companies' own reports) were searched by script for amendment numbers and are not otherwise read. No independent reader had read this correction at its release; the thirtieth reading has since read the counts, before the amendment's text was found, and found that no count needs changing. The thirty-first reading read that text.

### Tie lines on Anthropic and OpenAI from pages another session saved (Nov 17, 2026)

Not a reading: the builder's own additions, from pages a reader working for it read whole. Checked by script only; no independent reader has read them.

- What the pages are. On Nov 17 another session of this project saved 32 pages on the positions of Anthropic, Microsoft and Amazon on chip export controls: company statements, a filing with the Commerce Department, two essays, product announcements, and news and trade articles of 2025 and 2026. They are not counted articles and no dot stands for them; one is of the fifteen days, an analysts' note of Nov 12, 2026 on Anthropic's prospectus, which names neither Nvidia nor Huang. A reader working for the builder (the same model: not an independent reading) read 30 of them whole and listed 235 statements and acts with their exact words, each checked by script against the saved page. Two pages hold only a site's bot barrier and were not read: by their addresses, an Axios article of Oct 16, 2025, and a page on the lifting of the controls on Anthropic's models.
- What is drawn, and why at once. The builder draws records about Anthropic and OpenAI as soon as it finds them: it is a model made by Anthropic, and an open record about either company should not wait on a decision of scope. Three tie lines gained parts and four are new (73 tie lines at that release). No dated act was added or changed: these are records of 2025 and 2026 from before the fifteen days, and one note of Nov 12. The builder read each passage on its page before writing it.
- Anthropic and Nvidia, before the affair. The line on their clash of May 2025 now gives Anthropic's advocacy through that year in its own words: its filing of Apr 29 ("Anthropic strongly supports" the diffusion rule), its call on Mar 6 for "tightening semiconductor export restrictions", "controlling H20 chips" among them, its concern on Jul 23 at "the Administration's recent reversal on export of the Nvidia H20 chips to China", and its chief executive's words of Oct 21 on "filling the PRC's data centers with US chips they can't make themselves". It gives Reuters's relay of the Wall Street Journal of Nov 13, 2025, that the GAIN AI Act "is also backed by AI startup Anthropic", with Reuters's caution that it "could not immediately verify the report" and that Anthropic did not immediately respond. The line on money between the two gives Anthropic's announcement five days later that "NVIDIA and Microsoft are committing to invest up to $10 billion and up to $5 billion respectively in Anthropic".
- A claim checked against its footnote. The line already quoted Anthropic's claim, as CNBC gave it, that smugglers had hidden chips in "prosthetic baby bumps". The footnote of its filing for that claim cites a press report of 2022 about "over 200 Intel Alder Lake CPUs": processors of another maker and of another kind. The line now says so, beside Nvidia's answer about "tall tales", and gives the filing's own wording of the second claim ("packing GPUs alongside live lobsters").
- A senator the figure draws. In the same statement of Oct 21, 2025 Anthropic's chief executive wrote: "We agree with leaders like Senators Tom Cotton and Josh Hawley that this would only help the Chinese Communist Party win the race to the AI frontier". The builder found the sentence while checking a passage; the reader had listed it and no row carried it. It is a new line between Anthropic and Sen. Cotton, and it says that the statement names no chip bill and no contact with either senator. Among the 235 lines of the reader's table it is the only one whose words name a member the figure draws.
- Huang and Anthropic's chief executive. The line that gave Huang's "Lunacy" now gives his remarks of June 2025 as Fortune reported them ("he believes that AI is so scary that only they should do it") and the company's answer that its chief executive "has never claimed that".
- A charge about Anthropic's motives, with its denials. A new line gives, from Fortune of Aug 18, 2026, an investor's account at second hand of something Anthropic's chief executive is said to have said, a former White House adviser's charge of "regulatory capture", the company's denial ("Complete and utter nonsense"), the adviser's point that the chief executive "himself did not directly address" the claim, and the chief executive's own reply; and, from the analysts' note of Nov 12, 2026, that "some have suggested these comments carry ulterior motives". The figure states no motive of anyone's: the line reports that the charge was made, by whom the pages say, and what was answered.
- The government and the two companies' models. A new line gives the Commerce Department's order of June 12, 2026 requiring a license to export two of Anthropic's models, its easing on June 26 and its withdrawal, from a law firm's note and a trade outlet; the line says that the model that builds this figure is of that family. A new line of OpenAI's gives, from the same trade outlet, that it "delayed the full public launch of its GPT-5.6 model at the government's request". The builder read that page for both companies.
- The register. Three open groups are now marked as worded in part: two on the fight over the GAIN AI Act in the defense bill of 2025 (the members' lines and the line between the two companies word the reports and the backing; that Nvidia, the industry and the White House pressed against the act, and that the House's version dropped it, is on no row), and one on the reversal of the block on the H20 chip in July 2025, which Anthropic's submission mentions. 838 groups are open as before, twenty-six of them worded in part.
- Not drawn. Microsoft's and Amazon's own positions and acts on these pages (among them, by Reuters's relay, Microsoft's public support of the GAIN AI Act and Amazon's support in private), officials' acts, and the reader's other lines: they are other actors' earlier acts and wait for the owner's decision on what the image carries. The reader's notes also question two rows the builder has not yet read again (the date of an essay on the line about Davos, and which bill a chip of the fifteen days means), and name a report about OpenAI's dealings with Nvidia that is not saved. No independent reader had read any of this at its release; the thirtieth reading has read these lines since.

### Three rows read again on the notes of the reader of those pages (Nov 17, 2026)

Not a reading: the builder's own changes. Checked by script only; no independent reader has read them.

- What this is. The reader of the 32 pages left notes on rows already drawn. The builder read each on its page after the release that drew the tie lines. Three rows changed; no day, actor or id changed, and no act or tie line was added.
- Nvidia's reply, fuller. The line on the clash of May 2025 gave Nvidia's answer without its first sentence, which is the company's argument. The Register's update of May 1, 2025 prints it as sent in response to Anthropic's note: "China, with half of the world's AI researchers, has highly capable AI experts at every layer of the AI stack". It is on the line now. An open group of the register holds Huang's own repeated statements of that claim; it stays open, because the line gives a spokesperson's sentence and not his.
- A primary for an analogy. The line that gives the comparison by Anthropic's chief executive of chip sales to China with "selling nuclear weapons to North Korea" rested on a relay of his remarks at Davos. An essay of his, which its page dates January 2026, has it in writing ("this is like selling nuclear weapons to North Korea and then bragging that the missile casings are made by Boeing"), and the line now cites it. Whether the essay and the remarks at Davos are one occasion or two is not shown.
- A dated report beside a support that had no day. The chip on Semafor's sentence that Anthropic "has publicly supported all three bills" said, rightly, that the page gives that support no day. It now adds Reuters's relay of Nov 13, 2025 that Anthropic backed the GAIN AI Act, which the chip's own page calls "an earlier version of the AI Overwatch Act", one of the three. The reader, who had only the 32 pages, had noted that they do not show whether that act is one of the three; the chip's page does.
- Not changed. The reader noted that a headline of Aug 18, 2026 may explain why two sums for OpenAI's arrangement with Nvidia differ by $145 billion; the article is not saved, and the line keeps both sums as its pages give them. No independent reader had read these changes at their release; the thirtieth reading has since.

### Two pages read from the Internet Archive (Nov 17, 2026)

Not a reading: the builder's own additions. Checked by script only; no independent reader has read them.

- What this is. Two of the 32 pages showed the reader only a site's bot barrier. The Internet Archive holds both (captures of May 26 and July 15, 2026); the builder saved them as new folders and read them whole. One capture arrived compressed, and its decoded page stands beside the saved bytes under a new name. Three tie lines gained parts and one is new (74 now); no act and no day changed.
- The charge about Anthropic's motives, from its first saved occasion. The line began with a page of 2026 that says an adviser "repeated" the charge. Axios, on Oct 16, 2025, has the first: the White House's AI adviser, answering an essay by Anthropic's policy chief, claimed that "Anthropic is running a sophisticated regulatory capture strategy based on fear-mongering". Axios put a question to each side, whether each was "being hypocritical", and the line gives both. It also gives what Anthropic's chief executive wrote five days later: "there has been a recent uptick in inaccurate claims about Anthropic's policy stances".
- Anthropic and the White House in October 2025. A new line between Anthropic and the president, beside the order of February 2026: Axios wrote that its chief executive "publicly supported Kamala Harris for president, and has been noticeably absent from White House tech events"; five days later he wrote of "Our alignment with the Trump administration on key areas of AI policy" and of "a good conversation about US leadership in AI" with the president at a summit.
- Where the order on Anthropic's models began. The line had it from a law firm's note that the order "followed outreach to senior government officials by a US company". The second page names the company: "The concern that triggered the order originated with Amazon", whose researchers reported prompts that could draw out information useful in cyberattacks; by its relay of the Wall Street Journal, talks between Amazon's chief executive and the White House helped prompt the order. Anthropic "disputed the severity". The line gives all of this. That page does not say what Amazon is to Anthropic; since the release after this one the line gives it from two other saved pages: Anthropic's own announcement of Nov 18, 2025 ("Amazon remains Anthropic's primary cloud provider and training partner") and a market page of Nov 10, 2026 (Amazon "has a $15 billion note agreement with Anthropic").
- OpenAI, from the same page. The line on OpenAI's delayed launch adds that it "said it had previewed their capabilities for the government ahead of the launch". Each of the two pages was read for both companies. No independent reader has read these changes: the twenty-ninth and thirtieth readings held a copy from before them.

## What is in this repository

| Path | What |
|---|---|
| `figures/` | the figure as .png, .jpg, .html |
| `research/nv8_rows.tsv` | every row the figure rests on (2,968), and the rows of 3 acts withdrawn after an audit had read them, which are kept and marked (2,971 records in all): the words drawn, the sources cited and the passage found in each |
| `research/evidence.tsv` | one line per cited source of every row: what it is, its link, the passage, when it was saved, and where a copy sits in this repository if one does |
| `research/coverage_rows.tsv`, `harvest.tsv` | the pieces on the story with the headline as listed and the saved aggregator page each was first seen on; every link harvested from those pages. Headlines are given word for word, so some name people the figure does not draw (a columnist in the headline of her own column, a reporter in a headline about a broadcast, and others: the tenth reading found at least four such headlines). They appear here, in the rows and evidence tables that repeat the headlines, and in the figure's dot labels. This is not a promise that the repository names no one else who is not drawn: the figure's own context lines name a funder and a company's staff member who are not actors, and the earlier readings' files and the credits in the source code name others |
| `research/coverage_day_pages.tsv` | for each piece, the timed capture of an aggregator's front page that dates it: the earliest one that lists its address |
| `research/member_ties.csv` | the records for members of Congress (the figure draws the `words` column) |
| `research/employee_gifts.csv`, `employee_gift_rates.csv`, `committee_keys.csv` | the FEC lines of gifts by people who gave Anthropic or Nvidia as employer (no names), the totals across the 535 members, and each committee with the member it is tied to |
| `research/joint_committees.csv` | every joint fundraising committee and leadership PAC that received such a gift and that the FEC links to no candidate: its participants as its statement of organisation lists them, whether it is tied to one member (only when exactly one sitting member is among the participants) or attributed to no one, and both companies' lines and sums either way |
| `research/lpac_keyed_check.csv` | the 51 leadership PACs tied to a member on the evidence set's key, each compared with the sponsor its own FEC record names |
| `research/employee_gift_negatives.csv` | the negative receipt lines (refunds, chargebacks) on the same committees: listed, not taken off the sums |
| `research/coverage_scope_read.tsv` | the links the word rule left out that a reading put on the story (counted) or could not settle (left out), with who read each and on what basis |
| `research/lobbyist_gifts_map.csv`, `lobbyist_gifts_map_summary.tsv` | gifts to the members drawn that lobbyists' contribution reports list, made while the firm was registered for Nvidia, for Anthropic or for a group Open Philanthropy funds: one rule for all three sides, each line with the address of its official report; `own_money` says whether the line is a lobbyist's own money (the only lines the figure counts: the report says SELF or gives the filer's own name and nothing else) or a political committee's contribution that the report lists (`contributor` names the committee, or says that it is a campaign committee carrying the filer's name, which is not printed because it would name the lobbyist; `repeat_of` marks one gift listed by several lobbyists); no lobbyist is named (each has a number) |
| `research/nv8_lookup_counts.tsv` | the counts behind the figure's sentences on analysts, bylines, banks and remarks (no names) |
| `research/nv8_name_search_counts.tsv`, `nv8_s6_counts.tsv` | the counts behind the sentences on the whole-name search of the look-ups and on the search of the actors' own channels (no names) |
| `research/nv8_s6_items.tsv` | the search of the actors' own channels, line by line: for each chip searched, whether its original was read, could not be opened or was not found, and why; for each actor, each channel and item found and what was done with it (`scripts/as_built/nv8_s6_counts.py` counts it) |
| `research/nv8_remark_verdicts.tsv` | the second audit's verdict on each remark of a drawn actor found on two or more pages: carried by a chip, in part, or not (keys only, no text) |
| `research/anthropac_by_recipient.csv`, `anthropac_party.csv`, `pacs_by_member.csv` | AnthroPAC's contributions by recipient and by party; company PAC money by member |
| `research/chip_bills.csv`, `lobbying_bill_reports.csv`, `lobbying_family_reports.csv` | the chip-export bills; the lobbying reports that name each bill by its number; and, since Nov 17, the reports that name each of the five measures by a bill number, by a Senate amendment's number, or by name only (the reports themselves, by their ids, are in the three-chairmen repository's `research/lobbying_filings.csv`) |
| `research/grant_rows.csv` | the yearly award rows that the grant totals in the figure are sums of (from the funder's public grants index) |
| `research/story_pages.tsv`, `nv8_articles.tsv` | the 1,324 saved pages by address: the article each is a copy of, whether it is counted and why not, its day and the words the day was read from, and the members its text names; no page text and no headline |
| `research/nv8_article_pairs.tsv` | every pair of saved pages that an address or the texts made alike, with the verdict of the reader who opened both (the same article or different) and the kind of relation; a verdict overrides the joining rule |
| `research/nv8_page_days_read.tsv` | the pages whose metadata and text carry no date, with the day read in the saved copy and the exact words it was read from |
| `research/nv8_pages_in_part_read.tsv` | every counted page that shows a sign of a subscription barrier or holds a short text, and one page a later reading found among the longer pages, with a reader's verdict on whether the saved copy holds the whole piece, and the verdict used where it differs (the reader's own sentences are not published). A page is whole when all of the listed page's own piece is saved, whatever it quotes or links to. The verdict is about the saved copy; the text read from it can still lack small parts (a photo caption, a subheading, a credit, a short sentence, a line inviting the reader to another report) |
| `research/nv8_text_added.tsv` | the pages where something of the piece that the text read lacked was added to it: a table or a photo caption left out when the text was taken from the saved HTML, lines of prose dropped at the page breaks of a page saved by hand as PDF, entries of a live page that the saved HTML holds, or a short paragraph |
| `research/nv8_text_trimmed.tsv` | two pages of one site whose text read also held the opening of the next article of the site's endless scroll, with the length read before and now |
| `research/nv8_collapse_screen.tsv` | every counted page whose text holds a collapse mark ("Show more", "Read more" and the like), with what the mark belongs to as read by the builder; one page's own entries are collapsed |
| `research/nv8_actor_days.tsv` | a second gauge of the chips: every drawn actor and day that the census readings carry (in two or more counted articles, or in one) and on which the actor has no chip, with the reason (the same act on another day or row, answered in the register, a request for comment that drew no reply, or not on the matter). The gauges match a census line to an actor by the speaker's name as the reader wrote it, and do not see a line under a name their list does not hold |
| `research/nv8_general_news_page.tsv` | for the one counted article that is a live page of general news: every drawn member it names, in how many entries, and in how many entries on the matter |
| `research/nv8_report_day_screen.tsv` | a screen of the acts placed on the day of their report (a dashed edge): each one for which a census line gives the same words another day, whose own source has a day word near a cited passage, or whose cited page was first published or changed after the chip's day; and, read from the saved pages themselves, each one a cited passage of which stands in an earlier page, is missing from another copy of its article, or comes from a page dated only by an update line; each with the builder's reading |
| `research/nv8_remarks_no_chip.tsv` | a third gauge of the chips: each group of direct quotations by a drawn actor that two or more counted articles carry, that shares no five words in a row with any chip of that actor and that the register of remarks has not judged, with the builder's reading. Groups the register had judged earlier, by a looser test, are left out of it; the builder read those separately on Nov 16 (the README's list of that day) |
| `research/nv8_refusals.tsv` | the instances behind the rule for refusals to comment: every act whose words carry a refusal, with its actor, day and whether it sits on its own day or its report's; and every line of the census readings that holds words of refusal by a drawn actor and stands in an article no refusal act of that actor cites, with the builder's classification of it (another outlet's relay, naming the act it repeats, or not a refusal to comment). A candidate finder: it searches a fixed list of words, and a relay is matched by what the page says was asked |
| `research/nv8_single_article_remarks.tsv` | what the third gauge does not list, read by an independent reader: every group of quoted remarks by a drawn actor that one counted article gives and no chip carried by its words (294 groups and 7 planted controls), with the first words of the line, the reader's verdict, and for each candidate the builder's disposition (the act it is drawn on, or the reason it is not) |
| `research/nv8_paraphrased_acts.tsv` | the paraphrased lines of every drawn actor that no chip carried by its words (4,976 lines in eight lanes), as independent readers sorted them into acts: each act with its number of lines, the reader's verdict, the reader's own description, and for each candidate the builder's disposition; two acts were held out of each lane's table as controls |
| `research/nv8_earlier_acts.tsv` | the 67 groups and acts that readers of the lanes had set aside as "outside the days" (said or done before Oct 29), read again in full by a further reader: each with the first reader's lane, the second reader's verdict and report day, and the builder's disposition (the act it is drawn on, the tie line that carries it, or why the reader's verdict was not followed); four acts were held out of that reader's table as controls, and the three that had an item came back |
| `research/nv8_recalls.tsv` | every line twelve further readers listed when they read the counted articles whole for reports of earlier acts (2,855 lines); 631 earlier passages of pages another session saved, as five further readers sorted them (pages outside the table of counted pages, but for ten lines on counted pages that the first readers had been given as tests; the first column says which); and 70 lines added since (by the builder after the twenty-second to twenty-sixth and the twenty-eighth readings and after the third round of readers of the other session's pages, nine of them from pages outside the table; and four from the reader of nine pages added on Nov 17), marked in the first column: the reader's lane, the page and its day, the actor, the verdict (carried, or a candidate occasion or part), the chip or tie line the reader named, the row proposed, the way the reader says the passage cuts, and the builder's disposition (drawn, carried elsewhere or in part, not moved with the reason, a planted control, or open), and the earlier act the line was sorted into. It holds no words of the pages |
| `research/nv8_take_in_groups.tsv` | the second iteration of taking in the pages another session saved: every group of passages of the fifteen days that a grouping reader called new, a part of a chip or unclear (193), four more groups on Anthropic and OpenAI, and the 32 records of a later reader of three articles, each with the lane, the actor, the grouping reader's verdict, the builder's disposition (new act, part of a chip, carried, no reply and so on), the chip it concerns, the day, whether that day is the act's own or its report's, the way the builder reads it, what the builder changed when it assembled the rows, and what was changed after an independent reading read the table; the passages are not published (`scripts/nv8_take_in.py` states the classes and recounts) |
| `research/nv8_take_in_round3.tsv` | the third round of taking in the pages another session saved: the 95 candidate records that three further readers, reading 89 pages from their saved HTML, placed in the fifteen days or gave no period, each with the lane, the record, the page, the actor, the first reader's verdict, the builder's disposition (new act, part of a chip, carried, relay, no reply, earlier act and so on), the chip or register act it concerns, the day, whether that day is the act's own or its report's, the way the builder reads it, and in a last column what changed after an independent reading (nine records after the twenty-ninth; the builder's first disposition stays beside it); the passages are not published (`scripts/nv8_take_in_r3.py` states the classes and recounts) |
| `research/nv8_added_pages_acts.tsv` | the 120 passages a further reader judged in nine pages saved by hand and added on Nov 17, and in two pages read with them and left out as later than the days: the page and the day it prints, whether it was added to the count, the actor, the day the page gives the act, the reader's verdict against the chips (carried, or a candidate occasion or part), the chip named, the way the reader says it cuts, and the builder's disposition (drawn, open in the register, a planted control, or not drawn with the reason). It holds no words of the pages |
| `research/nv8_recall_groups.tsv` | the register of earlier acts: the 772 groups into which six further readers sorted the open lines of that reading and its 30 control lines, each in its reader's words, 27 acts the builder has added since (ids that begin `B.`), in the builder's words, and 195 acts that five further readers formed from the earlier passages on pages another session saved (ids that begin `E`), each in its reader's words, with the actor, the date as the pages give it, the kind (statement, decision, event, money, other), whether the pages present it as first reported in these days or as public before, its earliest page and day, how many lines and pages recall it, the way its reader says it cuts, and the builder's disposition (carried, with the chip; open and worded in part, with the chip that words the part and what remains; or open). A group is not proof of one distinct act: some hold several occasions. It holds no words of the pages |
| `research/nv8_blind_spot_population.tsv` | the list behind the population numbers of the paraphrase lanes: every line of the census readings by a drawn actor that no chip carried by its words at one of three stages, or that a lane read, with its actor, page and article, the stages, and the lane that read it; it holds no words of the lines (`scripts/nv8_blind_spot_population.py` states the rule and recounts) |
| `research/nv8_population_chips_stage1.tsv`, `research/nv8_population_chips_stage2.tsv` | the acts as they stood before the first three lanes and before the six further ones, frozen so that the population can be recounted at each stage |
| `research/nv8_live_pages.tsv` | every saved page whose HTML holds a live-blog record: how many entries the HTML holds, how many of them the text read holds, whether the page shows a control that loads further entries, and the page's mark |
| `research/nv8_at_the_line.tsv` | everyone named or quoted in three or four counted articles: the articles, how many are separate reports on the matter, and whether the person is drawn |
| `research/member_selection.tsv` | how the members drawn were chosen: every name searched with the number of counted articles naming it, whether it is drawn and, if not, why; then every other word found after a congressional title in three or more articles |
| `research/coverage_scope.tsv` | every link harvested from the aggregator pages with the class the word rule gives it (`story`, `fallout`, `other`); only `story` is counted and drawn. The rule is `scripts/story_scope.py` |
| `sources/` | copies of sources that are official records or the builder's own captures |
| `audit/` | the audit's brief, report, findings and recounts, and the disposition of every finding |
| `scripts/` | `build_nv8.py` and `verify_nv8.py` run on this copy, with the row files the figure is drawn from (`rows_ties.py` and `rows_events.py` are shared with other figures of the same project and also hold their rows); `as_built/` holds scripts as they were run, which need the builder's evidence sets and downloads |

News reports, the companies' and other organisations' pages and posts are linked and quoted in a sentence; they are not
copied. Headlines are given as the aggregators listed them.

## Who made this

The figure, the tables, the scripts and this file were drafted with Claude, a model made by Anthropic, working at the
direction of the repository's owner. Anthropic is one of the subjects of the figure. For that reason every statement is
tied to a record a reader can open, donors and lobbyists are counted and not named, and the work was audited by another
company's model.
